# 13 — Implementation plan (handoff to the implementation orchestrator)

> This is the work breakdown for a Claude Code orchestrator and its agent team. Product scope: product/14 (v0.1 = M1 loop, M2 trust, M3 show-the-value). Every work package (WP) lists inputs, outputs, acceptance criteria and dependencies. The orchestrator should run WPs as tickets through its own process: plan → implement → review (fresh context) → tests green → merge.

## How the work is run
See `14-orchestration-protocol.md` (orchestrator loop, fresh-context implementer and reviewer subagents, ledger `PROGRESS.md`, commit/push policy).

## Ground rules for the implementer
1. Read `docs/README.md`, all `docs/product/*`, all decisions, then `docs/technical/*`. The docs are the spec; product questions go to `docs/OPEN-QUESTIONS.md`, not into code comments.
2. Follow the code structure in technical/01; dependency rule `domain ← application ← infrastructure ← apps`.
3. Tests per technical/10 are part of each WP's definition of done; contract tests for every integration port; golden fixtures for SDK streams.
4. No secrets anywhere; `.env.example` maintained with every new variable; gitleaks pre-commit.
5. Conventional commits with `Signed-off-by`; small MRs; each WP ends with docs updates (ADR if a decision changed; the changelog is the GitHub Release body `pnpm changelog --release-notes` renders from the conventional commits since WP-71, never a committed file).
6. Install `docs/technical/CLAUDE.md.proposed` as `CLAUDE.md` at repository root in WP-00 and keep it under 200 lines.

| WP-15c | **Webhook ingress and the `inbox`** — `apps/server/src/routes/` has no `/webhooks/<provider>/<integrationId>` endpoint (technical/06 § "Inbound") and no polling fallback, so nothing in production emits `ticket.matched` even once WP-15b lands, and `inbox` is unwritten for the same reason. Carries the **inbound redaction step** `docs/TODO.md` has open — `IgnoredDelivery.detail` and normalised payloads reach `events.payload` while TD-012 requires the redactor on every write to it — and records `redaction_count` on the inbox row. Unblocks the `KnowledgeIndexer` job (WP-18) **Architect ruling, recorded in PROGRESS under "Architect ruling (WP-15c)"**: the delivery key stays the adapter's **redacted plaintext** key — the one-way digest is **closed, not deferred** — because (a)'s safety property is a post-condition of the redactor and holds on every binding unconditionally, while a digest's weakness is conditional on deployment configuration an operator cannot see. The ruling struck the ledger's claim that `APP_SECRET_KEY` is unavailable (`config.ts:353`, `pipeline.ts:269` already derives it) and, more importantly, found that **the question was spent on the one column already guaranteed clean**: `inbox(headers, payload)` has existed unredacted since `0005_events.sql:113`, TD-012's write list does **not** name `inbox`, and **GitLab's legacy scheme sends the binding's webhook secret as plaintext in `X-Gitlab-Token`** — so an ingress storing the raw delivery writes a live credential to the database on every delivery, with nothing planted. `inbox` also has no `redaction_count` column, so this WP owes migration **0014**. | WP-15b, WP-08, WP-09 | a signed provider delivery to a running `apps/server` instance appends `ticket.matched` and drives a task to `task.completed` **without a seeded row**; a replayed delivery is deduplicated by `inbox(provider, delivery_id)` and performs nothing twice; an unverifiable signature is refused and audited; a binding credential planted in the delivery body reaches neither `events.payload` nor the inbox row; **and a matched ticket that has no task row is re-emitted, not merely detected** — since WP-15d the intake handler writes nothing and the task is created by the `pipeline.outbound` job it enqueues through `afterCommit`, which is at-most-once (TD-004), so a crash between the two leaves a matched ticket that nothing re-emits, nothing retries and nothing logs (PROGRESS backlog entry 20 has the evidence: no poller exists — `ports/integrations/task-management.ts:157` is the only mention — and `event-bus.ts:377-392` logs only a callback that *threw*). This is **compatible with the dedup criterion above because the two name different units**: dedup is about a **delivery**, recovery is about a **task**. So the reconciliation finds matched tickets with no task row and appends a **new** `ticket.matched`, leaving `inbox(provider, delivery_id)` untouched — re-dispatching the *old* event is **not** an option, because `handlerExecutions.claim`/`complete` (`event-bus.ts:351`/`:370`) commit in the handler's own transaction and a replay of that position is skipped by design. Safe to run blind: intake is idempotent on `tasks_project_id_ticket_key_mode` (`saga.ts:204`). Asserted by dropping the `afterCommit` callback and showing the ticket still reaches `task.completed` **and** `inbox.headers`/`payload` are stored `redactJson`-redacted, applied **after** `verify` and **after** the key is computed, with the signature verdict **persisted** because a redacted payload can no longer be re-verified; `redaction_count` on the row is the **summed** count over all three redactions, not the key's alone, which is ~always 0 and would be a dead signal; asserted live by a GitLab **legacy-token** delivery whose stored `inbox.headers` does not contain `webhook_secret_token` and whose `redaction_count >= 1`; and `packages/integrations/src/providers/delivery-key-redaction.test.ts` is left **unchanged**, because `expect(key).toContain(MARKER)` is the half that fails both when an adapter forgets to redact and when a new provider keys a field its recipe does not plant into |
| WP-15d | **Outbound provider calls move out of the handlers' transactions.** Three pipeline handlers call a provider while `context.scope.tx` is open — the intake default-branch read (`saga.ts:215` → `:261`/`:265`, in `pipeline.intake` at **priority 10**, the core band), the workpad upsert (`workpad.ts:168`) and the status transition (`workpad.ts:218`) — so a pooled connection is held across provider latency and the audit write nests inside the caller's transaction (`postgres-unit-of-work.ts:55` takes a second `pool.connect()`). This contradicts CLAUDE.md's stage shape (*transaction / no transaction / transaction*) and technical/06 § "Outbound: actions" (line 370: actions are triggered by handlers **in the integrations band 100–199**). It is the **cause** of two things WP-15b met as symptoms: `integration_actions_task_id_fkey`, which made every e2e die on its first event because the audit commits before the saga does (the FK was **correctly** dropped and is not reopened here); and the pool arithmetic — `POOL_RESERVATIONS.pipeline = 2` (`config.ts:220`) missed the nested connection, so a dispatch peaks at **3** against `CONNECTIONS_PER_DISPATCH = 2`, floor `2N+8` against a worst case of `3N+8`, and at the shipped default (N=1, `APP_DB_POOL_MAX=10`) **the floor equalled the default with zero slack**. WP-15b fixes the arithmetic (`auditPerDispatch`), which is the accommodation; this row removes the shape. Mechanism: `HandlerContext.afterCommit` + a job that re-validates on fire (TD-004, TD-005) — **not** a bare callback, which is at-most-once. Amend technical/02 and technical/06 to state the transaction an outbound action runs in. **Consequence under load is a hypothesis, not a measurement** (pool exhaustion / audit-write timeouts at concurrent intake); PROGRESS backlog entry 17 has the evidence | WP-15b; land **before or with WP-15c**, which is what first makes it live | no pipeline handler reaches a provider while a transaction scope is open, **refused mechanically** at `PipelineIntegrationsPort.forProject` so the next handler to try it fails a test rather than a production pool; the intake branch check, the workpad render and the status transition each survive a rolled-back handler transaction without having called the provider, asserted per site; a task whose commit succeeded and whose wake-up was lost still reaches the provider on the job's next fire; `POOL_RESERVATIONS.auditPerDispatch` is **0** and the pool floor is back to `2N + …`; and the exhaustion hypothesis is **measured** — N concurrent intakes against the shipped default, with the provider latency and the load stated (rule 64) — before the move as well as after |
| WP-15e | **Whole-row `tasks.save` loses a concurrent writer's update — the class, not the instance.** `TaskRepository.save` (`packages/application/src/pipeline/store.ts:80`) writes the **whole** row: correct for a saga step, and wrong the moment a writer exists outside the pipeline's own ordering — which WP-15d created in the ordinary course of moving a provider call out of a transaction, by rendering the workpad in a `pipeline.outbound` job that runs beside the stage executor's transactions. **Measured, not reasoned**: *"a bug ticket walked all seven agent stages and finished with `cost_actual` 2.40 instead of 2.80, because the workpad's whole-row write landed between the executor's read and its write and put a stale cost back"*, and in the same first `verify:e2e` after the move a feature ticket *"sat at `ci_gate` until the 90 s settle gave up"* — one cause, and the second symptom is the one that generalises: the loss reaches columns the racing writer never meant to touch, so a task simply stops moving with no error anywhere. WP-15d fixed **one** writer with a narrow port method (`saveWorkpad`, `store.ts:97`, one column, pinned by the contract case *"writes the workpad without writing anything else, so a concurrent cost survives"*); **twenty** production `tasks.save` call sites remain (`stage-executor.ts` 4, `transitions.ts` 5, `saga.ts` 11 — counted by grep, not measured for concurrency). Two shapes, and the choice is this row's to make rather than to assume: a **narrow write per writer** (cheap, no migration, does not compose — every new writer needs a reviewer to notice it needs one) or **optimistic concurrency on the row** (a version column, a refusal, a retry — catches the writer nobody anticipated, costs a migration and an error path per site). It is a silent data-corruption class whose first instance was found only because a cost happened to be summed, and what it corrupted is the platform's own record of spend. PROGRESS backlog entry 18 has the evidence | WP-15d; **before** any further work package moves a task write into a job | a `save` that lands on a row another transaction has moved since it was read **cannot silently win** — either it writes only the column it owns, or it is refused and the caller retries — asserted by a test that reproduces the interleaving **deliberately** (a job writing a task row beside a stage-executor transaction; WP-15d's shape, no load needed, rule 66); `cost_actual` **and** `stage` each survive a concurrent writer, asserted per column because the two observed symptoms were different columns; and every remaining `tasks.save` call site is either converted or covered by the refusal, with the count stated in the change rather than left to a reader to recount (rule 63) |
| WP-15f | **The ticket's own words — the platform reads no ticket text, so the first agent stage is given a key and a URL.** `tasks` stores `ticket_provider`/`ticket_key`/`ticket_url` (`0004`:6-8) and nothing else about the ticket, `ticketRefSchema` is `{provider, key, url}`, `ticket.matched` carries no title, and `TaskManagementPort.readTicket` (`packages/application/src/ports/integrations/task-management.ts:179`) — which already returns `title`, `description`, `comments[]`, `epic`, `siblings` and `attachments_text` (`:76-95`) — has **no production caller**. So the two things built to consume the ticket's content get its identity instead: the prompt's task block is three lines, `provider:`/`key:`/`url:` (`packages/domain/src/prompt/assembly.ts:342-351`), against technical/04 § "Prompt assembly" step 5's *"Task block: **the ticket**, artifacts, return feedback…"*; and the retrieval query at the first agent stage is the ticket key alone — `extractQueryTerms('ACME-1') -> ["acme"]`, `extractQueryTerms('PROJ-1234') -> ["proj","1234"]` — against technical/07:11's *"task text (ticket + spec)"*. **The documents are right and the code is behind them**, so nothing is amended here; this is work. Measured at WP-17 and confirmed by its reviewer independently of the implementer that reported it; PROGRESS backlog entry **23** has the evidence, and **Q61** is the product half (where the text lives) with a recommendation strong enough to build from: a bounded, redacted `ticket_snapshot jsonb` on `tasks` beside the `template_snapshot` precedent, fetched once at intake through a **fourth `pipeline.outbound` duty** beside `intake_check`/`workpad`/`status` (`packages/application/src/pipeline/jobs.ts:69`) so the call is outside every transaction and through `IntegrationActionExecutor` (WP-15d). **Nothing in the repository can currently fail because of this** — `FakeClaudeRunner` picks its scenario from `spec.stage` (`packages/infrastructure/src/runner/fake-claude-runner.ts:96`) and never reads the prompt — which is why it survived to WP-17 and why this row's acceptance is written against the *assembled prompt* rather than against a run's output | WP-15c, WP-15d, WP-17 (all landed); **Q61** for the storage shape; land it **before** the first real-model run, because it will otherwise be read as a prompt-quality problem | the assembled prompt at the **first** agent stage contains the ticket's title and description **inside** the `kind="ticket"` data block, asserted against the assembled prompt and not against the pack, so WP-17's delimiter contract is held at the same time; `taskTextOf` (`packages/application/src/pipeline/planner.ts:190`) yields the ticket's own words — stated as *"a ticket titled 'rollback sessions after a failed migration' produces a query containing `rollback`, `sessions`, `migration`"* and **never** as a relevance claim, because the fixture vault cannot falsify one (backlog 16); the snapshot is fetched by a `pipeline.outbound` duty and written by a **narrow** repository method — **no new `tasks.save` call site**, because a job writing a task row beside the stage executor is exactly backlog entry 18's interleaving and this must not become its twenty-first instance; the snapshot is **bounded and redacted** with the cut declared on the row (a `truncated` marker) and `redaction_count` recorded, and the byte budget is **stated in the change** rather than left to a reader to infer (rule 63, and Q54's *bound at the consumer* — here the store is the consumer); a manual start (`POST /api/projects/:id/tasks`, which writes no `inbox` row) gets a snapshot by the same path as a webhook-started task, asserted; and a ticket whose title carries a zero-width character is still retrievable by that word, or the residual is **stated at the line** (backlog entry 12's measured half: `extractQueryTerms('sess<U+200B>ions rollback') -> ["sess","ions","rollback"]`) |
| WP-15g | **Compose a production `ClaudeRunner` and the run's workspace into `apps/server` — WP-15a's shape a second time, and no plan row owned it.** `apps/server/src/pipeline.ts:430` composes `composition.runner?.(platformTools) ?? unavailableClaudeRunner()`, whose `start` **throws** `RunnerUnavailableError` (`:113-115`), so the first real ticket to reach an agent stage fails its run and escalates the task (Q59). Every green result in this repository is green through `FakeClaudeRunner`, which picks its scenario from `spec.stage` (`packages/infrastructure/src/runner/fake-claude-runner.ts:96`) and never reads the prompt — **standing rule 82**, which is why the acceptance below is written the way it is. **Q52 was checked rather than assumed and is not a blocker**: its recommendation — *"build the launcher as a plain object and compose it in-process for now"* — is implemented (`apps/launcher/src/service.ts`, `runtime.ts`: `buildLauncher` returns `{config, service, provider, logger, stop}`), and its two open sub-decisions (Unix socket versus HTTP; one interface versus a narrower remote surface) are both about the **out-of-process** transport, so neither blocks an in-process composition. **It is not pure wiring, and four collaborators have no production implementation at all** — said plainly so nobody scopes this as an afternoon: (1) **nothing builds a `WorkspaceSpec`** — the only producer in the tree is the test fixture `packages/infrastructure/src/workspace/fixtures.ts:38`, and `agenticConfigSchema` (`packages/contracts/src/config.ts:219`) has **no** workspace section, so limits, the egress allow-list (technical/05 § "Network policy": the model provider host, the git host, the registries discovery found), `runtime`, `readOnly` and `keepUntil` must be derived from instance config and the git binding under BD-025's narrow-never-widen rule; (2) **the runner has to be built per run** — `ClaudeRunnerDependencies.spawnClaudeCodeProcess` is a construction-time dependency (`packages/infrastructure/src/runner/claude-runner.ts:140`) while the socket path and token are per run (`WorkspaceAttachment`, `packages/application/src/ports/workspace.ts:188`), and `ClaudeRunner.start` is **synchronous** (`ports/runner.ts:431`) while `create`/`attach` are not; the shape to copy is WP-15a's loader, which already builds adapters **per call** for the same reason (Q55); (3) **no production `RunTranscriptSink` exists** — `run_messages` has existed since `0006_transcripts.sql` and nothing under `packages/` or `apps/` writes or reads it (grep finds only the contracts DTOs and docblocks), and the e2e passes `sink: { append: async () => {} }` (`test/e2e/support/pipeline.ts:433`), so a real run today produces a transcript nothing stores and no screen shows; (4) **no production `ToolApprovalPort`** — `apps/server/src/platform-tools.ts`'s own docblock names what is missing (*"the Question aggregate's HTTP surface and a waiter for the human's answer, the transcript sink, the task read model"*) and eight of the nine platform tools are named refusals. Two further facts this row owns. `@platform/launcher` exports **only** `./src/index.ts`, a `#!/usr/bin/env node` entrypoint with a top-level `await main()`, so importing the package in order to compose it **starts a launcher**: the service needs an export of its own (or `LauncherService` moves into `packages/infrastructure`). | WP-19 | Cost ledger, rollups, budgets projection, price table maintenance job, estimates. **Also owns the backfill WP-15a's sweep makes necessary**: `run.finished`/`run.failed` are declared `unconsumed` in `EVENT_CONSUMPTION`, so the outbox sweep completes them and a ledger registered later starts empty. Flip both rows to `handled` **and** build a re-dispatch that replays a range of `events` into a newly registered handler (`events` is append-only, and the payload carries `usage`, `model_usage`, `cost`, `num_turns`, `wall_ms`, so nothing is lost — it just has to be read back). See `docs/TODO.md` and TD-005's WP-15a amendment. **It also owns `run_model_usage`'s writer, which no row named before**: nothing in the tree inserts into that table, so `model_usage` on the run DTO is `[]` for every run (`apps/server/src/queries/pipeline-queries.ts:196`, projected by WP-15h), while `run.finished` carries the per-model record in its payload and `normaliseModelUsage` already builds the rows — its docblock names this work package for the 1-hour-cache write it deliberately under-attributes in a multi-model run (`packages/infrastructure/src/runner/transcript-normaliser.ts:185-193`) | WP-04 | reconciliation test: sum of entries = rollups; budget pause behaviour; **a run that finished before the ledger's handler was registered appears in the rollups after a backfill**; **a finished multi-model run's per-model usage is readable through `GET /api/runs/:run_id` rather than `[]`**, from rows the ledger wrote rather than a seeded table |
| WP-20 | Web app foundation (TD-013): auth screens, layout, theme, realtime client, Query bridge, board, task detail, run detail with transcript renderer, agents view, inbox, settings, integrations setup, pipeline settings, budgets, statistics, audit log, knowledge browser + proposals | WP-06 | Playwright e2e with fake SSE; bundle budget check |
| WP-21 | Onboarding wizard steps 1–5 incl. discovery agent and readiness evaluation (product/06, /17) | WP-16, WP-17, WP-20 | wizard completes on fixture repo; readiness report produced |
| WP-22 | Docker images (base, runtime, egress, launcher, product), Compose (local mode as a `compose.local.yml` override — TD-018's amendment says why a profile could not express it), `image.yml`, `base-image.yml`, size checks, attestations. **Also owes the three measured things PROGRESS backlog 7 carries** (the `User nobody` / uid-1000 mismatch in `renderEgressConfig`, `apps/runlet` bundled to one file with `scripts/runlet-container-check.mjs` re-run against the real image, and the missing env companion for `WorkspaceImages.egressCommand`), and **is the package that lets the real provider be tested against the real images** (PROGRESS backlog **27**) | WP-14 | `docker compose up` on a clean VM yields a working instance; images signed; **and the real images replace the stand-ins in the tier that already runs the real provider** — `test/e2e/workspace/docker-workspace.e2e.test.ts` runs against `platform-runtime` and `platform-egress` with `runtimeSourceDir` **unset** (no `/repo` bind mount, which technical/05 forbids in a run container, and therefore with the shim started from the image's own entrypoint) and with `egressCommand` empty, and it is CI's `e2e-fake-claude` job that runs it — a green `node scripts/runlet-launcher-check.mjs` or `runlet-container-check.mjs` does **not** satisfy this criterion, and neither does a case that skips when the image is absent: **a skip counts as a failure here**, so `image.yml` publishing a tag the e2e job pulls is part of the criterion rather than a follow-up. **Amended after WP-22 measured it** (refiner, session 5): the criterion is met by a **stronger** route than the one it names, and the row says so rather than leaving a sentence the change does not literally satisfy. CI's `e2e-fake-claude` job **builds** `platform-runtime` and `platform-egress` from the commit under test instead of pulling a published tag — it therefore tests *this* commit's images rather than the last tag pushed, and needs no registry round trip — and the no-skip half is enforced where it belongs rather than by a registry being reachable: `ensureImages` **throws**, naming `node scripts/build-images.mjs runtime egress`, when a `platform-*` image is absent, so a missing image is a failure and can never become a skip. `image.yml` still publishes the tags; nothing in CI's e2e path depends on that publication, which is what removes the ordering between the two workflows. Within that tier, **the readiness handshake is asserted against the real provider by a case in which the shim begins listening after `attach` was called** — calibrated in both directions the way the unit half was (shortening `#waitForControlSocket` (`packages/infrastructure/src/workspace/provider.ts:785`) to a single look fails that case **by name**; unmutated it passes) — because `create` returning and the socket listening are different instants and the real image changes the gap between them, which is the one thing the stand-in cannot measure. And **the rendered egress config starts the real sidecar**: asserted by a proxied request served through `platform-egress` running as uid 1000 with `cap_drop ALL`, not by the contents of the file `renderEgressConfig` wrote |
| WP-23 | Docs: operator guide (install, integrations setup, upgrade, backup), user guide, `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md` | WP-22, **and after WP-15i and WP-15j** — the user guide describes task and run commands that must exist and an instance whose `/` must answer the SPA; written earlier it documents two 404s | a stranger installs in < 1 hour (dogfood test) |

## Milestone M2 — trust
WP-24 review-only mode; WP-25 ticket readiness linter; WP-26 rebase gate + conflict warnings; WP-27 steer + take-over/hand-back (export, resume instructions); WP-33 nightly real-LLM smoke + evals in CI (`llm-ci` environment). **WP-28…WP-32 have rows of their own in the table below** (written by a refiner in session 5, at `785941f`): they were one-line items with no acceptance criteria while M1's rows carried them, and each one turned out to have a shipped half, a half nobody owned, or both — the estimate exists and its gate does not, the autonomy dial is a word in a column nothing reads, and the whole Slack notify band technical/02 attributes to the finished WP-10 has no open owner at all.

**WP-27 additionally owes the 14-day retention window** for a paused or taken-over workspace. `buildWorkspaceSpec` writes three days at create time because nothing then knows how the task will end (`packages/infrastructure/src/workspace/spec.ts:169,187`) and no path ever relabels the volume, so technical/05:10's *"14 days for paused/taken-over"* is unimplemented: a taken-over workspace is purged on day 4 with the human's work in it. The evidence is PROGRESS backlog **7**'s retention bullet; acceptance is that a workspace whose task was taken over survives the sweep past day 3 and is purged after day 14.

*Refiner (session 5): **met by WP-27's `extendRetention`**, pending that row's merge — and the sentence above is wrong about why it had not been done. A relabel was never available: measured against Docker Engine 29.7.2 / API 1.55, `docker volume create` with an existing name and a different label **succeeds and keeps the original labels**, and `docker volume update` answers "can only update cluster volumes". The implementation therefore writes a second, marker volume `hold-<run-id>` and the sweep takes the later of the two instants; the criterion is unchanged and is asserted through the shared contract suite against both the fake and a real daemon (held past day 3, removed after day 14, never shortened). **technical/05 owes a sentence naming `hold-<run-id>`** — the measurement and the amendment are in PROGRESS backlog **7**'s retention bullet. Four things WP-27 did **not** carry are backlog **67–71**: the misrouted return-reason channel (67, live defect), the export's download surface (68), `paused_reason` plus product/19 § 19's unbuilt 5-working-day timeout (69), the take-over/hand-back screen (70, cheapest by adjacency on **WP-30** if it is wanted in M2) and the run workspace's missing checkout branch (71, a criterion for whoever composes a production provisioner — **Q52**).*

| WP | Title | Depends on | Acceptance criteria |
|---|---|---|---|
| WP-28 | **The estimate is shipped and its gate is not — `requiresBudgetApproval` is defined, unconsumed, and reads a threshold no query can answer.** WP-19 already built the number half of this row: `costEstimateHandler` runs on `artifact.created` at TD-005 priority 15 and writes `tasks.size` and `tasks.estimate_usd` through the narrow `CostStore.saveEstimate` (`packages/application/src/cost/estimate.ts`, `packages/infrastructure/src/cost/postgres-cost-store.ts:417`), over the model in `packages/domain/src/cost/estimate.ts` (`estimateTaskCostUsd`, `estimateAccuracy`, per-size weights `S:1 M:2 L:4 XL:8`). So what this row owns is **the gate**, and the gate's function exists with no caller: `requiresBudgetApproval(preset, estimateUsd)` at `packages/domain/src/policies/autonomy.ts:219-220`, and a grep over `packages/` and `apps/` finds the definition and nothing else. PROGRESS's WP-21 note 4 states it in those words — *"`requiresBudgetApproval` is still unconsumed, and that is WP-28's… This row sets the dial; it does not build the gate the dial configures"* — and Q65's closing sentence says the same. **The threshold it would read is not stored**: `PUT /api/projects/:project_id/config` calls `applyAutonomyPreset(level)` and puts exactly two of the preset's fifteen fields (*sixteen* until WP-62 corrected the count) into a `human_actions` params blob (`apps/server/src/routes/onboarding.ts:568-580`, `probation_tasks` and `plan_approval`), while `projects` stores only the **level** — so nothing can answer "what is this project's `budgetApprovalThresholdUsd`", which is why WP-30 comes first. **The mechanism is a second `kind` on an existing path, not a new one**: `approval_kind` already contains `'budget'` (`packages/infrastructure/src/db/migrations/0002_enums.sql:94`), `planApprovalGate` is a working precedent keyed on `(task, kind, stage, attempt)` (`packages/application/src/pipeline/saga.ts:551-618`), and `decideApproval` plus `POST /api/tasks/:task_id/approvals/:approval_id/decide` shipped at WP-15i. **The estimate does not reach the workpad** although product/18:31 says it is *"shown in the workpad"*: `renderWorkpad` prints `Cost so far: <x> of <y> USD` and nothing else about money (`packages/application/src/pipeline/workpad.ts:132`). **And the number's own basis is thrown away**: `saveEstimate` writes two columns while `basis` (`project_history`, `org_history`, `unknown`) and `samples` are only logged (`packages/application/src/cost/estimate.ts:96-106`), so an approval card cannot say whether the figure rests on this project, the organisation, or nothing. A *budget row* (BD-010's org and project caps) also has no production writer — `insert into budgets` occurs twice in the tree and both are test files — but that is **WP-30's** step-4 item 4, named on its row; this gate reads the dial's threshold and needs no `budgets` row. **What it costs to leave**: product/19:111 publishes a *"Budget approval threshold"* of $20 at Assist and $50 at Supervised, and the UI will print it as a policy while nothing enforces it; product/09:27's *"Estimate before spend"* stops at "estimate", and the only thing that actually stops spend before a stage is the per-task cap the executor checks against project configuration | WP-19, WP-15i (both landed); **WP-30 first** — it is the row that stores the materialised threshold this gate reads, and a gate that re-derives it from the level at decision time violates BD-027:14. Backlog **50** (a cancelled run's spend reaches no `cost_entries` row, so both the accuracy metric and any budget window understate — Q70 (b)). Q65 (the estimate model, implemented). **Blocked on human: Q71** — which number crosses the threshold and what happens when there is none — filed with a recommendation strong enough to build from without an answer. Node and Postgres only; no daemon | **(1) The gate is a `kind: 'budget'` approval on the path that already exists.** Created in the saga beside `planApprovalGate`, keyed through `approvals.forStageAttempt` so a re-refinement does not ask twice — *(**the mechanism half of this clause was falsified at implementation and is superseded; the property is the one shipped**, refiner note, session 5: the two clauses cannot both hold, because `enteredAttempt` (`packages/domain/src/aggregates/task.ts`) increments on **every** re-entry, so an attempt-keyed lookup asks again on attempt 2. Canaried: swapping the shipped lookup back to `forStageAttempt` fails `saga.test.ts` › *"does not ask again when the task goes round refinement a second time"* by name and nothing else. The gate keys `(task, kind)` through a new port method `ApprovalRepository.latestOfKind`, added to the shared contract suite in the same change (rule 23) — a plan approval is a statement about *a plan*, which is re-made, and a budget approval is a statement about *the estimate*, which is written once. `planApprovalGate` keeps `forStageAttempt` and is **correct** to: checked by the refiner against the same re-entry mechanism, a second plan is a second thing to approve, and the contract suite pins both questions)*, decided through WP-15i's existing decide route, with **no second route and no new task state**. Asserted from **both** sides (rule 42): a task whose estimate is above the threshold waits, and one at or below it is not gated — a gate that fired on everything would pass the first half alone. **(2) No estimate means no gate, and the task says so.** `estimateTaskCostUsd` answers `usd: null, basis: 'unknown'` for a project with no finished task, and that must not be read as zero (rule 16) or as *gate everything*; asserted with an empty-history project, and the absence is named on the workpad and the task DTO rather than rendered as a blank. **(3) The threshold is read from stored, materialised policy.** The change names the column or declared configuration key WP-30 writes and reads *that*; a test proves BD-027:14 by editing `AUTONOMY_PRESETS` in the source and showing an existing project's gate does not move. **(4) The estimate reaches the workpad, with its basis.** A forward-only migration (TD-011, next number after 0019) adds the basis and sample count beside `estimate_usd`; `renderWorkpad` prints the estimate beside `Cost so far` and stays a pure function of the task row; the edit goes out through the existing `pipeline.outbound` workpad duty, never from a handler, and `events/open-transaction.ts` keeps refusing the alternative. **(5) Approved and rejected both have an ending, asserted as countable effects and not as status codes** (rule 79): approval resumes the stage the gate suspended, rejection moves the task to a state the aggregate decides, each leaves exactly **one** `approvals` row, one event and one `human_actions` row, and a replayed decision leaves none extra. An expired budget approval follows the plan gate's own timeout behaviour rather than inventing a second one. **(6) The estimate is never re-made and the accuracy is published.** The write-once guard (`estimateUsd !== null`) is asserted across two refinement rounds; `estimateAccuracy` appears on the task DTO, and product/19:99's *"Cost estimate accuracy"* is computed from `tasks.estimate_usd` and `tasks.cost_actual` and from nothing else. **(7) Driven in the e2e tier against a real `apps/server` instance, with a fake runner that picks its scenario from the spec rather than from `spec.stage`** (rule 82) — the case needs a run whose recorded cost differs from the estimate, which a stage-dispatching fake cannot express. **(8) Rule 83: the sentences this falsifies are found by grep and corrected** — PROGRESS's WP-21 note 4, Q65's last sentence, the docblock at `packages/domain/src/policies/autonomy.ts:219`, and `EVENT_CONSUMPTION` if a new type appears |
| WP-29 | **`human_time_entries` has existed since migration 0007 and has never held a row — the projector technical/03 names does not exist, and one of the four kinds product/19 defines cannot be stored at all.** The table is `packages/infrastructure/src/db/migrations/0007_cost.sql:110-122` with indexes on `(task_id)` and `(user_id, started_at desc)`, typed in Drizzle at `packages/infrastructure/src/db/schema/cost.ts:126`, and technical/03:88 describes it as *"derived from events by a projector"*. There is no projector: a grep for `human_time` over `packages/` and `apps/` returns the migration, the Drizzle table and the enum, and no reader or writer. **The enum cannot express one of the four kinds**: `human_time_kind` is `('review', 'question', 'approval')` (`0002_enums.sql:122`) while product/19:145 defines *"steer = 5 min flat per steer"*; `run.steered` is declared `'unconsumed', // WP-27 steer` (`packages/application/src/events/consumption.ts:104`), so the fourth kind has neither a producer nor a value. **Two of the three review anchors have no event.** product/19:145 starts the review window at *"the first human MR activity (comment, approval, review start)"*; technical/02:130-131 gives the catalogue `mr.opened`, `mr.updated`, `mr.merged`, `mr.closed` and `mr.review.comment`, and **no `mr.approved` and no review-requested type** — `mr.review.comment` and `mr.merged` are `handled`, `mr.updated` is `'unconsumed', // WP-41 statistics`. **Per-user attribution has nothing to attribute to**: `human_time_entries.user_id references users(id)`, and `user_identities` — the only mapping from a provider's author onto a platform user (`0003_identity.sql:39`) — is **read** in exactly one place (`packages/infrastructure/src/integrations/postgres-inbox.ts:160`) and **written nowhere** in `packages/` or `apps/` — **a writer landed at WP-31** (`POST /api/org/identities`, admin; PROGRESS backlog **79**), so the null is now *unmapped* rather than *unmappable*: the table is empty until an operator maps an account and no screen calls either route, which is what this row must still assert against. So on this build every minute derived from an MR comment has `user_id: null`. **What it costs to leave**: product/09:29 and product/18:32 make *"total cost of delivery = tokens + people"* the platform's ROI argument and product/16:29 makes it a tracked metric; the dogfood plan's Phase B (product/19:160) records cost per merged task and can record only the token half. The statistics dashboard is **not** this row's — `GET /api/org/stats` has no route, no DTO and no rollup, and `apps/web/src/features/statistics.tsx:35` says so on the screen; that is WP-41's in M3. This row owes the **entries** | WP-19 (landed — the cost half the minutes sit beside, and `events/replay.ts`, the backfill that lets a handler registered today be served events swept last month). WP-27 for the `steer` kind and for a `run.steered` consumer; the other three kinds do not wait on it. WP-41 consumes the entries in M3 and does not block. **Blocked on human: Q73** — no document gives an hourly rate, so the single summed number cannot be computed honestly; the recommendation is to ship minutes and USD unsummed and gate the sum on an optional org setting. Node and Postgres only | **(1) One projector, one writer, idempotent under redelivery.** Handlers at declared TD-005 priorities write `human_time_entries` and nothing else does (a census in the shape of `tasks-column-ownership.test.ts` if a second writer is ever plausible); dispatching the same event twice produces the **same** rows, asserted by counting rows rather than by a return value (rule 79). **(2) Every cap and exclusion of product/19:145 is its own criterion, asserted at the boundary and one unit inside it** (rule 42): the 8 h per calendar day cap, *"excluding gaps > 2 h"*, 30 min per question, 10 min flat per approval, 5 min flat per steer — for each, a case at the value and a case past it, since a projector that always clamped would pass a one-sided test. **(3) The kinds the enum cannot express are decided in the tree, not dropped.** Either a forward-only migration adds `'steer'` to `human_time_kind` (TD-011: a new numbered file, never an edit to 0002) or the change states why steer minutes are not counted and product/19:145 is amended; and the review window names the event that supplies each of *"comment, approval, review start"* or states plainly that it starts at the first `mr.review.comment`, with the residual — a reviewer who approved without commenting contributes zero minutes — written at the code rather than implied. **(4) `EVENT_CONSUMPTION` is updated for every type consumed and the sweep-readiness gate stays satisfied.** If the projector needs `mr.updated`, that entry's `// WP-41 statistics` comment is now wrong and is corrected in the same change (rule 83); a type that stays unconsumed keeps naming the work package that will flip it. **(5) An unmapped identity stores `user_id: null` and the totals are still right.** Asserted with an event whose author matches no `user_identities` row — which is *every* event on an instance where nobody has mapped an account — the table has had a writer since **WP-31** and is empty by default, so both directions are now assertable: a mapped author attributes, an unmapped one stores `user_id: null` — and product/19:145's *"totals per task always shown"* holds in the presence of nulls; the per-user breakdown defaults **off** (product/18:32) and the default is asserted as well as the override. **(6) Minutes and dollars are two fields and are never added by this row** (Q73). The DTO carries both; no code path multiplies minutes by a rate this build does not have. **(7) The backfill works.** The projector is registered through `events/replay.ts` and a replayed range of `events` produces rows **equal** to those a live dispatch produced — an equality, not a spot check — and a second pass is a no-op, exactly as the cost ledger's backfill is. **(8) Driven from real events in the integration or e2e tier, never from a seeded `human_time_entries` table** (rule 82): the fake must produce the MR and question events the projector folds, or the tier certifies nothing about the fold |
| WP-30 | **The autonomy dial is a word in a column that nothing reads, and BD-027's one consequence — materialise at selection time — is the half that was not built.** `PUT /api/projects/:project_id/config` accepts `autonomy_level`, refuses a document whose `policies.autonomy` disagrees with it, writes `projects.autonomy_level`, and calls `applyAutonomyPreset(level)` — whose result reaches **only** the audit row's params, two of fifteen fields (*sixteen* until WP-62 corrected the count; `apps/server/src/routes/onboarding.ts:500-583`, `probation_tasks` and `plan_approval` at `:568-580`). BD-027:14 requires the opposite: *"Preset tables are versioned; changing a preset definition in a release never silently changes a project's effective policies (they are materialised at selection time and the UI offers 're-apply preset')."* Because only the word is stored, editing `AUTONOMY_PRESETS` in a release changes every project's policies at once. **Nothing in the pipeline reads the dial.** The one gate that could is `planApprovalGate` (`packages/application/src/pipeline/saga.ts:551-618`) and it reads `config.pipeline.template_overrides[…].plan_approval` and `size_threshold` — not `AutonomyPreset.planApproval`, not `probation`, not `planApprovalForRiskClasses`; `requiresPlanApproval` (`packages/domain/src/policies/autonomy.ts:195`) is defined and unconsumed exactly as `requiresBudgetApproval` is. So `picksUpNewTickets`, `stopAfterStage`, `businessReview`, `humanMrRounds`, `knowledgeAutoApply`, `reviewOnly` and `shadowMode` each have **no reader**, and product/19:111's preset table is a document rather than a behaviour. **Step 4 is one of five sub-steps.** `apps/web/src/features/onboarding.tsx:327-393` is four radio buttons and the `PUT`; product/18:50-54 asks for five things in that one step — dial, feature toggles with product/19:124's card fields, risk classes proposed from the repository structure, budgets including a separate shadow and maintenance budget, and notifications (channel, quiet hours, digest). **There is no project settings page for the mirror to live in**: `apps/web/src/features/settings.tsx:1-9` is *org* settings and its own docblock says autonomy defaults, provider mode, global budgets and feature flags *"need `GET/PATCH /api/org`, which no work package has built"*, while product/10:21 specifies the project settings page as *"autonomy dial, features (mirror of the wizard), WIP limits, policies, risk classes, budgets, Slack channel, quiet hours"*. **Budgets have no production writer at all**: `insert into budgets` appears exactly twice in the tree and both are test files (`test/integration/cost/postgres-cost-store.integration.test.ts:126`, `test/e2e/support/pipeline.ts:894`); the read is served (`apps/server/src/routes/projects.ts:163`) and technical/08's own unbuilt list names `GET/PUT /api/projects/:id/budgets` — so BD-010's org and project caps are inert on a real instance and WP-19's admission guard reads an empty table. **Which mirror this row owns was explicitly left to its planning and is decided here**: PROGRESS's WP-21 bullet says *"Say which mirror when WP-30 is planned: BD-028's wording covers step 4's settings, product/18:55 covers all five steps"* — product/18:55 is the broader and unconditional statement (*"Settings pages mirror the wizard one-to-one, so nothing is only reachable during onboarding"*), so this row owns **all five**, which makes the integrations create and test buttons this row's too, as that bullet already attributed them (both endpoints exist since WP-21; `apps/web/src/features/integrations.tsx` says at the top that they are driven from the wizard). **What it costs to leave**: the product's single explainable control does nothing — a maintainer who moves a project to Autonomous gets the behaviour of Observe — and the audit row records that the dial *meant* something it did not; every choice made in the wizard is reachable only during onboarding, which product/18:55 forbids | WP-21, WP-20, WP-15i (all landed). Backlog **52** (`human_actions` has eighteen writers and no reader but the idempotency guard, while product/18:5 requires *"Every toggle records who changed it (audit)"* to be **visible**) and backlog **53** (the SPA mints a fresh `Idempotency-Key` per request, so a double-submitted settings form is two writes). Q69 bounds it: a project may only *narrow* the organisation command maximum, so no dial level can widen what a run may execute. **Land before WP-28**, which reads the threshold this row stores, and before WP-32, whose channel, quiet hours and digest are step 4's item 5. **Blocked on human: none** — product/19:111 and product/19:124 give every default. Node and Postgres only | **(1) The materialised preset is stored, versioned and read back.** Selecting a level writes every `AutonomyPreset` field plus `AUTONOMY_PRESET_VERSION` where a query can read it (a column or a declared key of `projects.config`), and a test proves BD-027:14 directly: change `AUTONOMY_PRESETS` in the source, re-read an existing project, get the **old** policies; invoke "re-apply preset", get the new ones. **(2) *Custom* is computed from the stored preset, not from the level**, through the existing `describePresetOverrides`, published by the API and listed in the UI (product/18:9) — asserted both ways (rule 42): a project with no override is **not** shown as Custom. **(3) At least one policy the dial sets is enforced by the pipeline, and every one that is not is listed.** `planApprovalGate` reads the stored preset through `requiresPlanApproval` — including probation and risk classes — instead of only `template_overrides`; each remaining field with no reader is enumerated in the tree beside the work package that will read it, the way `EVENT_CONSUMPTION` enumerates an unconsumed event (rule 18: the absent case must not be the quiet one). A change that stores fifteen fields (the row said *sixteen*; corrected at WP-62) and enforces none fails this criterion. **(4) Step 4 carries all five of product/18:50-54, or names each missing one as a gap on the screen** — the shape step 3 already uses at `onboarding.tsx:320-325`, not controls that silently do nothing. Feature toggles carry product/19:124's card fields: name, one-sentence value, default, cost implication, what it touches externally. **Review-only mode is the first of those cards with a shipped behaviour behind it and no control anywhere** (WP-24, session 5): the mode is configured only in `.agentic/config.yml` or `projects.config`, and what turns it on is BD-028's opt-in key — `features.review_only.enabled` plus `trigger` (`label | all | paths`), `label`, `paths`, `severity_floor`, `max_findings` — **not** `AutonomyPreset.reviewOnly`, which this row's criterion 3 lists among the fields with no reader. The card writes the opt-in key; if it also drives the preset field, say which of the two wins, because today they are two switches for one feature and only one of them is wired. Two reads this card depends on are defects this row will meet first: backlog **58** (a stored `trigger: manual` makes `GET /api/projects/:id/config` — the read step 4 opens with — answer `500 invalid_stored_config`, naming no key) and backlog **55**. **(5) `PUT /api/projects/:project_id/budgets` exists and is the row WP-19's guard reads.** It takes `Idempotency-Key` performing nothing twice, writes one `human_actions` row per accepted write and none for a refused one, answers 401 per route and at least one wrong-role 403, and an **end-to-end** assertion shows a budget created over HTTP pausing a task through `cost/guard.ts` — because today `insert into budgets` exists only in two test files, an assertion against a seeded row would certify nothing (rule 82). **(6) The mirror is checked, not claimed.** A census in the shape of `apps/server/src/routes/client-census.test.ts` compares the wizard's command set against the settings screens', fails in **both** directions so the list cannot go stale, and names any deliberate omission in the test itself; the integrations create and test buttons are part of the comparison. **(7) Every toggle is audited and the audit is readable** (product/18:5, BD-003): each write records actor, before and after, and the change is visible through an audit read rather than only in a row nothing queries (backlog 52). **(8) Readiness only ever suggests.** `suggestedAutonomyCap` caps the suggestion and never the choice; a maintainer's visible override is accepted (product/18:18, BD-026, Q21), asserted with a readiness-level-0 project set to Autonomous. **(9) Rule 83: grep for the sentences this falsifies** — `settings.tsx:1-9`'s docblock, step 4's text in `onboarding.tsx`, PROGRESS's WP-21 bullets about the integrations buttons and about which mirror this row owns, and the "unconsumed" notes in `packages/domain/src/policies/autonomy.ts` |

This module is what that decision will run on."* Nothing schedules that queue in any composition root. **The configuration half exists and is short one field**: `features.digest.{enabled, at, quiet_hours{from,to}}` is in the strict config schema (`packages/contracts/src/config.ts:190-199`), and product/18:33's third configurable — *"urgent classes (escalation, budget 100%) still immediate"* — has nowhere to be stored, so urgency would be hard-coded. **And `resolveIdentity`'s destination has no writer**: `user_identities` is read at `packages/infrastructure/src/integrations/postgres-inbox.ts:160` and inserted nowhere, so a Slack reply cannot be attributed to a platform user. **What it costs to leave**: product/03:17-25's journey is a person watching a ticket and a Slack thread. On this build the only surface is the SPA, which WP-15j is still making reachable at all; every `task.question.asked` and every `task.escalated` waits for someone to open a browser, and BD-006's one-working-day question timeout expires against a human nobody told | WP-10, WP-15d, WP-15a (all landed: the provider and its digest mechanism, the `pipeline.outbound` duty shape every notification must use, the binding loader this row extends to a third type). **WP-30** for the wizard's step 4 item 5 and the settings mirror — channel, quiet hours, digest. WP-27 and WP-31 each add a notification class and neither blocks. Q42 (TD-024 names `@slack/bolt` and `@slack/web-api`; WP-10 shipped without them) is already answered in the tree and does not block. **Blocked on human: none** — product/18:33 and product/19 give the defaults: digest on at 09:00 org time, quiet hours off, urgent = escalation and budget 100 %. Node and Postgres only; no daemon | **(1) A communication binding resolves, both ways.** The loader answers a `communication` port for a project that has one and `null` for a project that does not — the shape `git: null` already has (`loader.ts:15-29`) — while a binding that **fails to load** throws rather than degrading into silence (rule 20's asymmetry is about an inbound delivery, not about a misconfigured credential on an outbound path). Slack joins the shipped registry and `shipped-registry.ts:8-13`'s sentence is corrected in the same change (rule 83). **(2) Every notification is sent from a `pipeline.outbound` duty and never from a handler**: a handler decides, the job calls, and `events/open-transaction.ts` refuses the alternative on both the handler path and the job path, so no provider call happens with a transaction open. **(3) The notify band exists and is declared.** Each type consumed flips in `EVENT_CONSUMPTION` with its handler at TD-005 priority 210, `sweepReadiness` stays satisfiable for a process whose `ROLE` sweeps (`all` or `worker`), and every type still `unconsumed` names the work package that will flip it — including the correction of the two budget entries, which name a finished work package today. **(4) Quiet hours defer and never drop, asserted from both sides** (rule 42): a non-urgent notification raised inside the window is delivered in the next digest and is not lost, and one raised a minute outside it is delivered immediately — both with an injected clock, never a wall clock (rule 2), and both boundaries asserted at the instant and one minute either side. **(5) Urgent classes are configuration, not a constant**: `features.digest` gains the field product/18:33 names, defaulting to escalation and budget-100 %, with the default asserted **and** an override asserted. **(6) The digest is idempotent on a countable effect.** The job run twice for one day posts **one** message through the existing `slack:digest:<channel>:<day>` key and an empty day posts **none**, both read back from `integration_actions` rather than from the port's return value (rule 79). **(7) A shadow-mode project posts nothing and records `would_have`.** Every notification and the digest carry the task's `mode` so the executor's shadow guard can refuse (`digest.ts:20-22`), asserted with a shadow task; a shadow project whose digest posted would be the defect BD-017 and the executor exist to prevent. **(8) Nothing a notification writes carries a secret.** Message bodies, the audit row and the idempotency key's parts go through the binding's redactor (TD-012), and a key that would need redacting is **refused** rather than redacted, for the reason `idempotencyScopeFor` states — redaction is many-to-one and a key is an identity. **(9) Exercised against the Slack fake through the real registration and the real loader**, so the rows, the decryption, the config validation and the redactor composition are production code and only the far side of the HTTP call is a double — the shape `apps/server/src/pipeline.ts:193-199` already describes for the e2e tier. **(10) Needs measurement** (rule 66, not run by the refiner): no tier has ever driven a notification from a pipeline event to a provider call, so the per-event volume a real project generates — and therefore whether an unbatched notification path is usable at all before quiet hours exist — is unknown. **(11) The notification *channel* control is this row's, and the dependency above runs the other way** (added by a refiner after WP-30, session 5). The "Depends on" cell says *"**WP-30** for the wizard's step 4 item 5 and the settings mirror — channel, quiet hours, digest"*; WP-30 shipped and built digest and quiet hours as stored keys and **named the channel as a gap on the screen**, for a reason that is this row's and not that one's: *"A channel is a property of a `communication` binding, and this build resolves `git` and `task_management` only — `CommunicationPort` has no caller anywhere (WP-32) … the channel would be a field whose value nothing could ever read"* (`apps/web/src/features/operating-mode.tsx:29-32`). So the control cannot exist before criterion 1 does, and done includes it: a channel chosen from the project's resolved `communication` binding on `features/operating-mode.tsx` — the **one** component the wizard's step 4 and the project settings page both render — with the gap panel **deleted** in the same change, which is what `apps/server/src/routes/settings-mirror.test.ts` then holds in both directions. Where the value is stored is this row's decision and it is not free: `features.digest` has no channel key today, and a channel is per binding rather than per feature |

## Milestone M3 — show the value
WP-34 shadow mode (closed tickets) + ShadowReport + UI; WP-35 history bootstrap; WP-36 maintenance pipeline; WP-37 risk classes + reviewer routing (CODEOWNERS); WP-38 dependency policy + Checks panel (licence/maintenance status); WP-39 coverage delta; WP-40 epic split (spike variant); WP-41 statistics deep-dive (readiness attribution, clean-first-MR rate, estimate accuracy); WP-42 release 0.1.0 (release-please, changelog, migration notes).

**The nine rows below were written by a refiner in session 5**, from the same reading M2's rows got at `785941f`: each was a one-line item with no acceptance criteria, and each turns out to have a shipped half, a half nobody owns, or both — the shadow *guard* is complete and nothing ever sets `tasks.mode = 'shadow'`, the coverage number is on three schemas and no delta exists, `dependency_policy` ships with a default and no reader, and `readCodeowners` and `listMergedMergeRequests` were built at WP-09 and have never been called. **Recommended order** (dependencies, then cost): **WP-37 → WP-39 → WP-38 → WP-34 → WP-35 → WP-36 → WP-40 → WP-41 → WP-42**. WP-37 and WP-39 are the two cheapest and each gives the Checks panel an item, so WP-38 builds that panel once rather than three times; WP-34 is the milestone's headline and the one row that needs the production runner path (Q52) exercised; WP-41 must come after WP-34, WP-36 and WP-29, because three of its inputs are **wrong** today (PROGRESS backlog **57**, **75**, **63**) and a statistics row that lands first publishes the defect as a number; WP-42 is last and additionally waits on **WP-33**, M2's remaining row.

**Three backlog entries have no home in the nine rows and are not silently attached to one.** PROGRESS backlog **78** (nothing opens the Socket Mode connection the shipped Slack manifest selects, so every inbound chat decision reaches no door) is **not** an M3 feature at all: it completes WP-32's journey, its own entry says *"this is not a backlog-sized change — composition, a lifecycle, a `ROLE` decision and an e2e over an injected socket — so if it is scheduled it wants a **row of its own**"*, and that row is **WP-43** at the next free number, with backlog 78 as its brief and backlog **79**'s remaining half riding with it. PROGRESS backlog **68** (the take-over export writes a tarball nothing serves and no transcript at all) is likewise none of these nine — it is **not** WP-41's CSV export, which is a different artefact — and its own entry says it is the same change as backlog **70** (nothing in the SPA calls take-over or hand-back), so the two together are **WP-44**: one route over WP-15h's existing transcript projection, one authenticated download behind WP-15j's realpath guard, and the screen that gives both a caller. **The third is WP-37's leftover**: PROGRESS backlog **91** (no review checklist exists anywhere in the product, so product/19 §14's two checklist classes ship as a refusal and a half-proposal) is **blocked on Q83** and is not a backlog-sized change — `policies.review_checklists` in the configuration document, `checklist:<name>` accepted by `riskRequirementSchema` with a class naming an undefined list refused at configuration load, the reviewer's prompt taking the project's items **in a data block**, and the Review Verdict recording which checklist was applied, which is a `ROLE_PROMPT_VERSIONS.reviewer` bump and eval cases (TD-016). No M3 row touches the reviewer's prompt, so that row is **WP-45** at the next free number, with backlog 91 and Q83 as its brief; WP-37 kept the affected classes' paths in `RISK_CLASS_REQUIREMENTS_AWAITING_CHECKLIST` so that finishing it is an edit of one table. The one piece of backlog 91 that needs **no** row and no answer to Q83 is the proposal telling the truth about `payments` — it is offered with one of its two documented requirements and is not in the screen's *"Not proposed, and why"* list. None of the three is scheduled here; all three are named so that the next orchestrator does not have to re-derive that no M3 row owns them. **A fourth, added by a refiner after WP-40 (session 5): PROGRESS backlog 108** — that row shipped no `apps/web` change at all, so the epic split has no acceptance panel (`GET /api/tasks/:id/breakdown` and `POST …/breakdown/decide` are served, guarded and named by hand in `routes/client-census.test.ts`, and no screen calls either) and no feature card (`FEATURE_CARDS` covers five of the nine shipped `features.*` keys, so no screen can turn `features.epic_split` on). It is **not a row of its own**: two of its three pieces are the task screen and the deletion of a self-expiring census case, which is exactly what **WP-44** already owes for take-over and hand-back — so backlog 108 rides with WP-44 and that row's acceptance criteria gain the breakdown panel and its two client functions. The third piece, the feature card plus the comparison of `FEATURE_CARDS` against `PLATFORM_DEFAULT_CONFIG.features`, is one edit of `apps/web/src/features/operating-mode.tsx` that backlog **72**'s note already owes two string edits in, and needs no row at all. **WP-41 and WP-46 are the wrong homes** for either piece.

| WP | Title | Depends on | Acceptance criteria |
|---|---|---|---|
| WP-34 | **Shadow mode on closed tickets, the ShadowReport and the Shadow screen — the guard is complete and the producer does not exist.** Everything that *refuses* a shadow task's writes shipped long ago: `taskModeSchema` is `('normal','shadow')` (`packages/contracts/src/common.ts:133`), `assertMutatingActionAllowed` throws `shadow.no_outbound_mutation` (`packages/domain/src/aggregates/task.ts:713-720`), `IntegrationActionExecutor` parses `mode` and returns `would_have` before the idempotency step (`packages/application/src/integrations/action-executor.ts:328`), and `runModeFor` maps `shadow` from `tasks.mode` (`packages/application/src/pipeline/planner.ts:555-562`). **Nothing ever sets it**: a grep for `mode: 'shadow'` over `packages/` and `apps/` returns **one** non-test match and it is a badge in the SPA (`apps/web/src/features/task-detail.tsx:450`); `tasks.mode` is chosen by whoever creates the task"* — *"the only pairing in this entry that is a single piece of work"*. **The screen says it out loud**: `apps/web/src/features/operating-mode.tsx:150-158` ships the card with the caveat *"The switch and its budget are stored; nothing in this build starts a shadow batch, so turning it on changes no behaviour yet."* **There is no Shadow screen**, although product/10:20 names one per project: `apps/web/src/features/` has fifteen screens and none is it. **The separate budget has a key and no enforcement carrier**: `features.shadow_mode.budget_usd` (`packages/contracts/src/config.ts:292-297`) against `budget_scope` = `('org','project','task','run')` (`0002_enums.sql:119`) and a guard that reads org and project only (`packages/application/src/cost/guard.ts:82`) — `operating-mode.tsx:519-524` already states the gap in prose. **What it costs to leave**: product/19:162's dogfood **Phase A** is *"onboarding + shadow mode on 10 closed tickets each; success = install < 1 hour, discovery draft accepted with ≤ 30 min edits, shadow similarity reported"* — the phase cannot start, and product/18:13's level 0 (**Observe**) is a dial position that runs nothing, which is the entry point the whole adoption argument rests on | WP-15g (the production `ClaudeRunner` and the workspace provisioner — with none composed, `startRuntime` installs `unavailableClaudeRunner` and a batch cannot run at all), WP-15c, WP-19 (the ledger, the admission guard and the task cap every shadow run must pass), WP-21 (the one-off-task precedent), WP-30 (the materialised preset this row's two policies are read from), WP-09 (`getMergeRequestDiff`, `listMergedMergeRequests`). PROGRESS backlog **72 (c)**. **Q52** for the run workspace, and backlog **71** (a run's workspace has no way to say which branch to check out) if the batch checks out a historical base. **Blocked on human: Q82** — what a shadow run is checked out against and how the human merge request for a closed ticket is found; filed with a recommendation strong enough to build from. Node, Postgres and a runner | **(1) A shadow batch is tasks, and there is no second entry point.** One `tasks` row per selected ticket on the ordinary ticket templates with `mode = 'shadow'`, so the admission guard, the cost ledger, the transcript sink, the budget cap and the escalation are the ones every run gets (`packages/application/src/onboarding/discovery.ts` carries the argument). It is the cheapest M3 row **not** to do and the most visible one to do badly — creating N tickets in somebody's tracker is the largest write this platform makes into another team's tool (A3's shape, and the reason every other outbound default in this build is conservative).
