## Milestone M4 — a real installation

M1 built the loop, M2 the trust surfaces, M3 the value story, and every row of all three is merged except **WP-33**. M4 is the milestone after which somebody who is not us can install this product, point it at their own GitLab and Jira, and let a ticket reach a merge request without an operator standing beside it. Its content is not new product: it is **what the delivery of M1–M3 discovered** — `docs/technical/PROGRESS.md` § "Open findings backlog" entries 0b–119, and `docs/OPEN-QUESTIONS.md` Q63–Q90 — grouped into rows an implementer can build in one fresh context. The disposition of **every** open entry and every Q63–Q90 is the table in `PROGRESS.md` § "Architect ruling (M4, session 6)", and each entry now carries a one-line owner note under its own heading. **A row here names what it folds and deliberately does not restate the entry's evidence: read the entry first, then the row.** An open question is implemented per its recommendation and never waited on; where a row implements one, the row says so.

**The order, and the argument for each group.**

1. **Money and state that cannot be recovered come first** (WP-47…WP-52), before anything a human can see. A dead run holds a budget reservation in *every future window* and the only lever an operator has loses the spend (backlog **109** with **50**); a lost wake-up bricks a history bootstrap that a unique index then refuses to restart (**106**); a poisoned event is re-dispatched twelve times an hour for ever (**43**); twenty of the thirty-six variables the server reads never reach the container, so a stock instance cannot create an integration at all (**54**); a binding's host is whatever the caller typed and the platform process has no egress control (**48**); and a run's structured output is stored unredacted and copied into a field the API serves (**35**). Four of those are graded **blocker** and the rest **major**. None of them is visible on a screen, all of them are cheap relative to what they cost, and every one of them gets *worse* the moment the next group lands.
2. **The run that makes them live** (WP-53…WP-56, and **WP-74**, which sits between WP-54 and WP-55 in the table rather than at the end: it is WP-53's criterion (5) finished, and it waits on WP-54 because WP-54 rewrites its predicate's input). Most of group 1 is latent for exactly one reason: `startRuntime` composes `unavailableClaudeRunner`, so no production run has ever executed. **WP-53 is the row that ends that** — it decides the launcher transport (**TD-028**) and composes the first production `RunWorkspaceProvisioner` — which is why it comes *after* the money rows and *before* everything else. Beside it are the three things a first real run would meet in its first minutes: a run that cannot execute one of its project's own commands (**49**, a blocker), a return that serves the stage a stale and unrelated complaint (**67**), and a blocking question or approval that parks a task for ever (**74**, **76**, **69**). **WP-75 and WP-76 sit after WP-74**, in that order: WP-76 gives the launcher a git credential so the developer stage can push and a private repository can be mirrored at all (backlog **133**, live since WP-53), and WP-75 must precede it because the whole-volume `/cache` mount (backlog **148**) is latent only while no fetch credential exists — the first private mirror would otherwise be readable from every other project's runs; WP-75 runs after WP-74 because both edit the same mount in `hardening.ts`. **WP-77 follows WP-76** (refiner, session 8): the revocation of a run credential whose runner died or whose teardown revoke failed (backlog **155**), which WP-76 filed rather than built as TD-028's amendment allowed.
3. **The record the platform keeps** (WP-57, WP-58). `run_context_pack` is cited as the platform's audit in three documents and has never held a row; retrieval's instrument cannot falsify its own precision. Both are about what a run can later be judged on, so they land once runs exist and before anybody tunes anything.
4. **Providers** (WP-59…WP-61, **WP-43**). The git port cannot close a merge request, reads a task's diff four times and invents nothing where the adapter is honest and the fake is not; two events two product documents promise are absent from the catalogue; the delivery metrics are wrong in two directions at once; and Slack hears nothing a human clicks.
5. **Screens** (**WP-44**, **WP-46**, **WP-45**). Three finished server features are unreachable from the product — the take-over export, the epic-split acceptance queue, the merge-readiness panel's remaining items — and one (checklists) is a product promise that ships weaker than it reads.
6. **Onboarding and the dial** (WP-62…WP-64): seven of fifteen dial policies set nothing while the screen publishes all fifteen as facts; the configuration a team agreed to never reaches their repository; readiness is frozen at the first discovery run and the wizard's step 3 does not exist.
7. **Operations** (WP-65…WP-67): the one budget that stops every project notifies nobody; a failed notification is invisible; a replayed command performs nothing twice and says it did.
8. **The instruments last** (WP-68…WP-73), because they are what stops the next session paying these costs again — guards whose scope is the wrong question, a harness kinder than production, a coverage budget with 0.05 points of headroom, a CI surface three documents describe and nothing runs, and the two-process deployment TD-028 makes the shipped topology.

**Parallel-safe sets** (different packages, no shared files). `{WP-50, WP-68, WP-70, WP-71}` touch `compose.yml`, `scripts/`, `vitest.config.ts` and `.github/` and are safe against every application row and against each other. `{WP-59, WP-60, WP-43}` are `packages/integrations` plus one composition root each. `{WP-44, WP-46}` are `apps/web` (they share `features/task-detail.tsx`, so run them in that order, not together). `{WP-63, WP-64, WP-65}` are onboarding, knowledge and notify and share nothing. **Serialise**: WP-47 before WP-48 (both write `packages/application/src/recovery/stranded.ts`); WP-53 before WP-54 (both write `packages/application/src/pipeline/planner.ts` and the workspace spec) and **WP-54 before WP-74** (WP-54 rewrites `TOOLS_BY_ROLE`, which WP-74's predicate reads); WP-52 before WP-57 (both write `stage-executor.ts`); WP-55 before WP-46 (the panel renders what the gate row stores); WP-60 before WP-61 (the metric consumes the event).

**What M4 deliberately does not contain.** Q52's *alternative* transports (Kubernetes, the launcher-relayed exec fallback of TD-025 §5) — TD-028 decides one and records the others as alternatives. Phase-2 search (TD-008's pgvector, TD-009's embeddings): nothing in the backlog asks for it and backlog **16** says the instrument cannot yet judge phase 1. A human "this bug belongs to that merge request" action (Q87 refuses it for 0.1). A second git or task-management provider: BD-017's point is that adding one touches no core row, and no open entry needs one. And **no new product scope**: every row here closes a gap against a document that is already accepted.

**Nothing in M4 waits on WP-33**, which stays M2's blocked row: it needs a model credential in CI and it is not this plan's to schedule. Three *measurements* named in the entries do need a real model — backlog **66** (does the review tail return after a conflict resolution), **100** (what a Reviewer pass over a human merge request costs), **102**/**103** (what a real history bootstrap reads) — and each is written into its row as "needs measurement", to be taken by WP-33 or by the first dogfood task, never guessed. WP-53 is explicitly **not** blocked on WP-33: it is verified against the real images with the scripted CLI, the way WP-22 was.

**Decisions taken inside rows** (so an implementer does not re-open them): the lease's owner and what a sweep may conclude from a missing heartbeat (WP-47); one `deadline.sweep` queue rather than four named ones, a stated deviation from TD-004 (WP-56); backlog 35's answer (b), a per-field artifact policy, over (a) (WP-52); backlog 67's answer (a), reader-side, over (b) (WP-55); backlog 31's answer (a), keep the table (WP-57); backlog 97's answer (a), state the audit's scope rather than widen the table (WP-51); backlog 25's harness-level answer over a bigger number (WP-69); Q89's answer, stop publishing `latest` (WP-71) — *superseded on 2026-09-16 by the owner's decision, Q96: `latest` stays as the newest push to `main`, and WP-71's criteria are rewritten from TD-019's amendment*. **One decision was too big for a row and is its own record: `docs/decisions/technical/TD-028-launcher-control-plane.md`.** Five product questions the rows raised are filed as **Q91–Q95** with recommendations strong enough to build from.

| WP | Title | Depends on | Acceptance criteria |
|---|---|---|---|
| WP-47 | **A run whose process died holds its budget for ever, and the only lever that frees it loses the money.** Folds PROGRESS backlog **109** (the lease sweep), **50** (a cancelled run's spend reaches no ledger row, rollup or budget), **110** (`runs.usd_estimated` has no writer, so in `local` provider mode every run commits nothing to any cap and reads as free on the wire) and **75** (`tasks.cost_estimated` is published and never written); implements **Q70 (b)**. **The architect's ruling on the lease's shape, so it is not re-derived.** *Who owns it:* the process executing the run writes `runs.lease_owner` (a per-process instance id) and renews `lease_expires_at` on a heartbeat inside the `stage.execute` job; both columns have existed since `packages/infrastructure/src/db/migrations/0004_pipeline.sql:90` with no reader and no writer. *What a sweep may conclude from a missing heartbeat:* **only that no process is renewing the lease** — never that the model stopped, never that the work was wasted. *What it therefore does:* ends the run with `run.failed` and a named terminal reason (`lease_expired`), never `cancelled`, so the stage executor's existing escalation applies and the ledger gets its chance; and it releases the reservation **with** whatever the run reported rather than with a zero. *The bound is both, not one:* the lease is the primary signal and `started_at + wallClockMs + grace` is the backstop for a row written before this column had a writer. The late-cost write is a **narrow** repository method (`runs.recordCost`) against an already-terminal row, never a whole-row save, and the ledger grows one branch for a late or estimated report that is **labelled** as one rather than merged into the original | WP-19, WP-15e, WP-15i, WP-36 (the recovery pass and its `recovery_attempted_at` mark); a migration for `runs.usd_estimated` becoming nullable and for the sweep's mark | **(1)** A `running` run whose lease has expired is ended by the pass and **not** by a human: integration tier, leave the row behind with the clock advanced, run the pass twice, and read back `runs.status`, the terminal reason **and** the scope's pending term (both, because the row moving is not the reservation moving). **(2)** A **live** run whose lease is being renewed is **not** swept — the negative case, same tier, rule 42. **(3)** A cancelled run's tokens reach `cost_entries`, `cost_rollup_daily` **and** `chargeBudgets`, asserted as a **sum across the task's runs compared with what the runner reported**, because WP-19's own invariant is true and blind here (rule 79). **(4)** `runs.usd_estimated` is nullable and written when `cost.is_estimate` is set; the pending fragment is `coalesce(usd_reported, usd_estimated, 0)`; one `local`-mode run's figure appears in the pending term and on `GET /api/runs/:id` and equals what the ledger charged the same run. **(5)** No zero is ever published as a cost (rule 16): a run with no price row still gets no ledger row and a named log line, unchanged. **(6)** `tasks.cost_estimated` is either a **projection** over `cost_entries where is_estimate` or removed with `docs/technical/03-data-model.md` in the same change — never left as a `not null default 0` column nobody writes; asserted at both boundaries (a task with one priced and one reported run publishes the priced amount, not the sum). **(7)** The measurement rule 39 asks for: the change states, from the shipped `DEFAULT_STAGE_RUN_BUDGET_USD`, **how much one stranded `implementation` run reserved before the fix ($15) and after it (nothing)**, and pins the release in a test rather than in prose |
| WP-48 | **Two sites of the lost-wake-up class remain, and the seam built to prove the class cannot reach either.** Folds backlog **106** (a lost `record` enqueue bricks a history bootstrap exactly as the resolved **101** did, and the three worker runtimes are composed with the *unwrapped* `Jobs` while the docblock lists one of them as covered) and **36** (a lost curation wake-up loses one task's proposals). Two halves, one change: the **sites** are two more rows of `packages/application/src/recovery/stranded.ts`'s table, and the **seam** is handing the wrapped `jobs` to `composeKnowledgeIndexing`, `composeOnboardingRecording` and `composeHistoryBootstrap` in `apps/server/src/runtime.ts` — or stating at the line why a worker's own enqueues are out of scope. Backlog 36 additionally needs the thing its entry says it needs before a re-enqueue is safe: a **mark that the curation happened**, because a curation that ran and proposed nothing is spelled exactly like one that never ran, and an idempotency key on `artifact_id` for the `knowledge.proposals` job, whose queue is `standard` today. **Two more, added by a refiner in session 6 from WP-47's discovered work — the ask-shaped half of the lease sweep.** Backlog **121** is a **fourth row of the same table**: an ask `pending` whose attached run is *terminal*, which the third row cannot see because it keys on a pending ask with **no** run attached, ended through the `endAsk` / `recordRefusal(failed)` that row already uses — no migration, no new port method, and no `recovery_attempted_at`, because this ends a row rather than re-enqueuing one. Backlog **120** is **not** this row's file and is folded here for one reason, that it is one session's thought with 121: an ask's run claims no lease (`packages/application/src/ask/executor.ts:415`), so the sweep reaches it only through the hour-long wall-clock backstop instead of the six-minute lease bound, and closing it is the claim the stage executor makes at `packages/application/src/pipeline/stage-executor.ts:764` plus the `lease` option at `apps/server/src/pipeline.ts:853` | WP-36 (the pass, the store, the grace period and the log line), WP-35, WP-18b, WP-47 (same file — run after it; and its `RunLeaseOptions`, which backlog 120 passes to a second composition) | **(1)** Both new rows are asserted the way the class's criterion mandates: **drop the enqueue** in the e2e tier and read the recovery back from the row's own status (`history_bootstrap_batches.status`; the `kb_proposals` rows for the curation), never from a return value. **(2)** Bounded: each site honours the resolved backlog **105** bound — one recovery attempt per row, recorded, with an ending when it is spent — so no site pays for a retry a minute. **(3)** Idempotent in both directions: a second pass over a recovered row is a no-op, and a curation re-enqueued after a partial write produces **one** set of proposals, not two. **(4)** The seam covers the three worker runtimes **or** the docblock at `apps/server/src/runtime.ts` states why not; the false sentence in its current list is corrected in the same change (rule 83). **(5)** A census, not a list: the test that enumerates which compositions get the wrapped instance reads the call sites off disk, so a fourth runtime is a decision somebody makes rather than a line somebody adds. **(6)** The entries' own trigger is honoured — no production deployment composes a provisioner **until WP-53**, so this row's e2e is the only proof it will get before then, and it must therefore be the tier that runs. **(7)** Backlog **121** is asserted the way the table's other rows are: end the run **through the sweep**, then read the **ask's own status** back rather than a return value, with the negative case beside it (rule 42) — an ask whose run is still live is untouched — and a second pass over an ended ask is a no-op. **(8)** Backlog **120** is closed **or declined in its entry by number**, never dropped. If closed: an ask run whose lease is being renewed is **not** swept and one whose heartbeat stopped is ended at the **lease** bound rather than at the wall clock — WP-47's criteria (1) and (2), asked a second time of a second composition — and the wall-clock backstop stays, because it is what covers every `runs` row written before migration 0035 |
| WP-49 | **An event whose handler always fails is re-dispatched roughly twelve times an hour for ever and blocks its stream.** Folds backlog **43** (no dead-letter state; WP-15e's conflict bound made a second door into the same room) and **5** (`SweepReport` cannot see a chained dispatch's failure). The clone helper (`:837`) and the mirror helper (`:542`) are scoped or their whole-volume mount is **stated with its reason** at the line (platform-only content). **(5)** `hardening.ts`'s WP-74 comment at the mount is rewritten (rule 83), and TD-021:9 is true **as written** — no amendment, and the change says so. **(6)** The residual is stated rather than implied: the git host on a run's egress list remains the exfiltration channel for the run's *own* project (backlogs **137**/**140**), and a mirror removed between the clone and the run container's create (no mirror GC exists on this build — **needs reading** before the statement is made) would surface as the daemon's sub-path refusal |

| WP-67 | **A command whose aggregate cannot refuse a repeat is performed twice under one key, and the dependency gate decides once and asks nobody.** Folds backlog **47** (`Idempotency-Key` is required, validated, audited and never consulted as a *record*: two simultaneous requests with the same key both perform, because both reads return null under READ COMMITTED and `human_actions` has no unique index), **99** (the shadow batch is the one command surface that does not answer the previous attempt, so a replay leaves two rows saying it did something), **96** (the gate is a one-shot duty with no re-entry: the `ask` ending gives up in seven task states and the `block` ending in four, and nothing re-attempts — so a blocked package can merge) and **92** (`tasks.requested_by_user_id` has no writer at any of the four task-creation sites, and at three of them the requester is in hand and dropped). **The product half of 96 is filed as Q91 with the entry's own recommendation** — leave `ready_for_merge` refused and keep the record — so only the human-owned stops are built | WP-15i, WP-21, WP-34, WP-38, WP-37; a discovery task created by a user carries that user and routes to their provider account when nothing else matched, and a task created by nobody still routes to nobody. **(8)** Rule 83: the three places that say the column has no writer are corrected |

Backlog **266** (the git port's self identity) is **not** in this row and is left for the M5 architect pass. |

## Milestone M5 — no side doors

M4 made a real installation possible: a stock instance starts, a private repository mirrors, a run executes its project's commands, and a ticket reaches a merge request. **M5 is for the paths around that front door.** Every way a person or a second process reaches the product other than the SPA's own button should meet the same gates, audits and records that button already meets. That covers a take-over at Ready, a repository file, a Slack reply, a Jira binding with no public URL, a second product container and a minted credential after its binding moves. **Why now, after M4:** M4's delivery found these paths once real runs, two processes and real bindings existed, and filed them as backlog 120–273. Most of them are live on the shipped topology today. Its content is **what M4 discovered**: every open entry in `docs/technical/PROGRESS.md` § "Open findings backlog", the un-numbered reviewer notes from WP-62 and WP-63, and the open part of `docs/OPEN-QUESTIONS.md`. The disposition of **every** one of them is in § "Architect ruling (M5, session 8)" at the end of `PROGRESS.md`, and each entry carries a one-line owner note under its heading. **A row names what it folds and does not restate the entry's evidence: read the entry first, then the row.** An open question is implemented per its recommendation and never waited on. Where a row does that, it says so, and the founder may reverse it.

**The order, and the argument for each group.**

1. **The side doors past a check come first (WP-79…WP-81)** because each one is live and each ships a change a check was supposed to see. A take-over at Ready carries a human's new commits into Ready without the platform's CI or rebase gate (**267**). A repository file can truncate a risk class's required reviewer out of the assignment (**273**). A minted push credential outlives the binding that minted it, and every process but one quotes it unredacted under a custom prefix (**156**, **259**). BD-024's tamper gate is a panel line that says *absent* (**95**).
2. **Money a run spends and cannot use (WP-82, WP-83).** A developer run is allowed the lockfile install and has no registry to reach, so it fails after spending its stage budget (**140**). A context budget above ~115 000 estimated tokens can exceed the model's window on a Czech vault (**173**). A return serves the stage a verdict that did not cause it (**159**).
3. **State the platform can lose, and the crossings of its own topology (WP-84…WP-86).** A deferred gate's wake-up, an undelivered organisation message and a question with no reminder (**240**, **236**, **165**) can each be lost. On `docker compose up`, steer and take-over cannot reach a live run (**134**). A split install refuses every enqueuing command until a worker has started, and a missing runner is invisible (**262**, **135**).
4. **Providers and chat (WP-87…WP-90).** A Jira binding without a public URL starts no ticket, and the one unauthenticated door has no rate limit (**187**, Q60). A Slack reply never reaches its task (**195**). The bug template's observability pre-fetch was never built (**143**). Three delivery metrics are wrong in a known direction (**191**–**193**).
5. **Configuration and the organisation (WP-91…WP-93).** Keys a project writes that nothing reads, a reading no run records, and an organisation with no settings document.
6. **Onboarding and screens (WP-94, WP-95).** Readiness is frozen on nine carried criteria (**230**, major). The dial does not reach maintenance (Q100). Five things the server publishes have no screen.
7. **The instruments and the documents last (WP-96…WP-98, WP-78).** These are what stop the next session paying these costs again.

**Parallel-safe sets** (different packages, no shared files). `{WP-82, WP-96, WP-97, WP-98}` touch `docker/`, `packages/infrastructure/src/workspace/`, `test/`, `scripts/`, `vitest.config.ts`, `.github/` and documents. They are safe against the application rows and against each other, except that WP-98 must not edit a document another in-flight row edits. `{WP-87, WP-89, WP-90}` are `packages/integrations` plus one application module each. **Serialise** these pairs:
- WP-79 before WP-85: both write `packages/application/src/pipeline/commands.ts`.
- WP-80 before WP-81: the gate's log excerpt needs the shape rule.
- WP-84 before WP-88: both write `packages/application/src/notify/`.
- WP-85 before WP-86: both write `apps/server/src/runtime.ts`.
- WP-91 before WP-92 and WP-93: all three write `packages/contracts/src/config.ts` and the settings port.
- WP-83 before WP-92: both change prompt assembly.

**What M5 deliberately does not contain.**
- `.agentic/pipeline.yml` and `pipeline.custom_stages`, which stay parsed, unread and reported `not_applied`. Q56 has no loop counter for a custom stage, and a custom stage that cannot return is a stage with no bound (BD-008). Only the **prompt** half of backlog 226 is here.
- Business review after an Assist hand-back (**Q106**) and scoping-only for a template with no architecture stage (**Q99**). Both would widen what a stage set runs, and that is the founder's call.
- The storage panel (**Q108**, recommended: amend the document).
- A second provider of any type.
- Phase-2 search.
- A git port *"which account am I"* member (backlog 266, declined for 0.1: the marker is anchored, and a forger only mis-counts a thread on their own merge request).
- The universal-ctags install **Q57** leaves open: it needs a pin and an image-size measurement, and nothing in the backlog measures what the map buys.
- **No new product scope.** Every row closes a gap against an accepted document, or implements a filed question's recommendation.

**Needs measurement, never guessed.** Five things need a daemon, a model or a real provider, and each is written into its row as *needs measurement*: backlog **136** (a replayed create across a launcher restart), **137** (the CLI's other egress hosts, which needs a model credential and so goes to WP-33), **149** (whether `tools` removes `Skill`), **171** (retrieval precision on a real single-subject vault, which the first dogfood vault answers) and **173**'s divisor (real token counts). Nothing in M5 waits on WP-33.

**Decisions taken before the rows**, so an implementer does not re-open them.
- **TD-028 amendment (M5)**:
  - decision **9**: a command reaches a live run through a `run_commands` row plus `pg_notify` to the lease holder, with the heartbeat as the guarantee (WP-85);
  - decision **10**: an unbound credential is revoked through the integration that minted it (WP-80).
- **TD-012 amendment (M5)**: a minted credential is redacted in every process by a recorded non-secret **shape**, never by a stored copy of its value. The shared exact-value registry is rejected for 0.1 (WP-80).
- **Decisions inside rows**:
  - WP-81: the tamper check is a deterministic part of `ci_gate`;
  - WP-82: an operator-declared registry host list, empty by default;
  - WP-83: a returned stage is shown only the verdict that caused its return;
  - WP-88: a chat decision writes its `human_actions` row in the delivery's transaction;
  - WP-91: an unread configuration key is reported at the settings write as it already is at the repository read;
  - WP-92: a project prompt is a **data block**, never platform text;
  - WP-93: a lowered organisation maximum applies at the next read and never to a frozen dial.
- **Filed for the founder:** **Q106**, **Q107** (implemented per its recommendation in WP-94) and **Q108**.

## Milestone M6 — the record and the run agree

M5 closed the side doors: every way a person or a second process reaches the product now meets the gates and audits the SPA's own button meets. **M6 is for the places where what the platform records, shows or promises is not what happened.** A cancel ends the record while the session keeps spending (**294**). A gate row reads *clean* where no check ran (**280**), and a task re-enters Ready on a rebase judgement older than its target (**274**). The settings screen refuses a stored configuration that the run path proceeds on (**311**). A run container outlives every record of it (**286**). technical/06 promises a merge-request poller, technical/08 a task export and product/08 a Sentry resolve-on-merge, and none of the three exists (**297**, **310**, **302**). **Why now, after M5:** M5's delivery found these once steer, take-over, polling, chat answers and the two settings documents were real, and filed them as backlog 274–333. Its content is **what M5 discovered**: every open entry in `docs/technical/PROGRESS.md` § "Open findings backlog", the un-numbered discovered work of WP-97 and WP-98, and the open part of `docs/OPEN-QUESTIONS.md`. The disposition of **every** one is in § "Architect ruling (M6, session 9)" at the end of `PROGRESS.md`, and each open entry carries a one-line owner note under its heading. **A row names what it folds and does not restate the entry's evidence: read the entry first, then the row.** An open question is implemented per its recommendation and never waited on; where a row does that it says so, and the founder may reverse it.

**No open entry is graded major or blocker.** Nothing filed in session 9 after WP-99's **279** and WP-96's **328** (both closed by their own rows) carries that grade, so the rule every M4 and M5 pass applied — every major has a row — holds with nothing to place. The one small-to-major entry still open, **171**, stays with the first dogfood vault, as M5 ruled. The order below is therefore by cost: money first, then checks that fail open, then the rest.

**Amended in session 10 (backlog 342).** The statement above held when it was written. WP-104 then found a major that every later row stands on: the run container's environment never reaches the `claude` process, so no containerised run has a route to the model (inferred, unmeasured). It gets a row, **WP-118**, placed immediately after WP-104 and ahead of WP-105…WP-117, per the architect ruling of backlog 342 in PROGRESS.md and TD-025's amendment of that session. WP-33 now depends on WP-118 as well as on a model credential.

**The order, and the argument for each group.**

1. **Money a human asked to stop, and a round nobody needs (WP-101…WP-103).** A cancel of a live run is never told to the session, which spends to its wall clock and is charged late (**294**); N processes serving the API admit N paid steers per user per window (**295**). Every task that edits an existing test pays a second `code_review` and `business_review` run, because the tamper check's confirmation is read only on a second pass (**Q109**, built as (a) at WP-81; M6 switches to (b)). A run container whose create answer was lost keeps running with no reaper, up to three per task on the slowest repositories (**286**).
2. **Checks that can be walked past, or that report what they did not see (WP-104…WP-107).** Four allowed test spellings, a `Write` to `.git/config` and a tracked directory symlink each put a write past the protected-path guard, and the content guard does not know the minted shapes (**281**, **282**, **283**, **277**). A stale rebase judgement, a tamper item with no check behind it, a verdict with no cause, a swallowed transaction guard and a shadow run started on today's tree instead of its base (**274**, **280**, **289**, **303**, WP-98's discovered work). A project setting cast rather than parsed on the run path, and a jobs-schema knob `migrate` never reads (**311**, **296**). A minted shape that reaches other processes up to 5 s late, and a binding credential committed to a prompt file that is stored and sent to the model (**276**, **316**).
3. **Work that is lost without a trace (WP-108, WP-109).** A lost reminder arm (**291**), a discovery task stranded `active` for ever (**320**), pg-boss's failed jobs (**325**), knowledge passes that lose a stream race (**333**) and a re-evaluation that queues the same page twice (**319**).
4. **Promises the documents make of providers (WP-110, WP-111).** The GitLab merge-request poller and edits on status-rule bindings (**297**, **298**); Sentry resolve-on-merge (**302**) and a Jira marker search that reads one page (**288**).
5. **The record and the surfaces (WP-112…WP-114).** The run and task record product/09 calls exportable (**309**, **310**, **293**); settings nobody sees until a run has spent money (**315**, **318**, **Q111**); the integration lifecycle and two operator screens (**331**, **332**, **324**, **327**).
6. **The instruments and the documents last (WP-115…WP-117, WP-78).** Three unmeasured query costs (**307**, **312**, **314**), three e2e tier gaps (**300**, **306**, **329**), and the documents. WP-78 stays behind its upstream StrykerJS precondition, and WP-33 behind a model credential; nothing in M6 waits on either.

**Row sizing.** No row folds more than five entries, and each touches one cause. M5's rows took two to three hours of implementer time plus one to three review rounds; these are sized the same.

**Parallel-safe sets** (different packages, no shared files). `{WP-104, WP-115, WP-116}` touch the command policy, the path guard, `test/`, `scripts/` and new migrations. `{WP-109, WP-110}` are the knowledge writers and the poll job. `{WP-101, WP-106}` touch `packages/application/src/pipeline/commands.ts` and the settings reads respectively. **Serialise** these pairs:
- WP-101 before WP-112: both change `packages/contracts/src/records.ts` and regenerate `schemas/`.
- WP-102 before WP-105: both write the rebase gate's settlement and `packages/application/src/pipeline/ready-head.ts`.
- WP-103 before WP-108: both add rows to `recovery/stranded.ts`'s table.
- WP-104 before WP-105: both write `packages/infrastructure/src/workspace/provider.ts`.
- WP-104 before WP-118 before WP-105: all three write `packages/infrastructure/src/workspace/provider.ts`, and WP-118 also writes `packages/infrastructure/src/runner/options.ts` after WP-104.
- WP-106 before WP-107 and WP-113: all three write `apps/server/src/knowledge.ts` or the settings port in `apps/server/src/pipeline.ts`.
- WP-110 before WP-111: 302's duty relies on the poller's `mr.merged` for a binding without a webhook.
- WP-113 before WP-114: both write `packages/contracts/src/api.ts` and the settings screens.
- WP-117 last: it must not edit a document another in-flight row edits.

A row that adds a migration takes the next free number **at merge**, in merge order; an applied migration is never renumbered or edited (TD-011).

**Needs measurement, never guessed.** Each of these is its row's criterion (1), taken before the fix is chosen (rule 27): a launcher restart during a create, a create that outlives the client timeout, and whether an unattached shim ever exits (**286**, a daemon, WP-103); whether a run's `core.fsmonitor` fires in `platform-runtime` (**282**, a daemon, WP-104); the run count a declared protected change costs (**Q109**, WP-102); which run-path keys fail open under a malformed stored value (**311**, WP-106); every `enqueueStage` site's after-commit loss and the census of job kinds that rely on pg-boss retries (**320**, **325**, WP-108); the librarian e2e at load ≈ 12 (**333**, WP-109); two query plans and an admission burst (**307**, **312**, **314**, WP-115). Still with WP-33 (a model credential): **137**'s CLI egress hosts and **173**'s divisor.

**Decisions taken before the rows**, so an implementer does not re-open them.
- **TD-028 amendment (M6)**:
  - decision **11**: a cancel of a run whose lease is live is a `run_commands` row the holder applies as `handle.stop`, and a run with no live lease is ended in place as today (WP-101);
  - decision **12**: the launcher gains one read verb listing the run ids of the containers and networks it labelled, `destroy` works for a container it holds no handle for, and the reaper is a runner-side recovery row, so the launcher still reads no database (WP-103).
- **TD-012 amendment (M6)**: a recorded shape reaches every process on commit, with the timer as the guarantee; the repository reading redacts prompt texts by the project's binding credentials before it stores them; the content guard uses the redactor's corpus (WP-104, WP-107).
- **Q109 switched to (b)** by the architect (reversible by the founder): the tamper check's confirmation is read in the rebase gate's settlement, in its transaction, not by a second pass through `ci_gate`. This amends WP-81's ruling that the check lives entirely inside `ci_gate`'s read (WP-102).
- **Decisions inside rows**:
  - WP-101: the steer window is shared through the `run_commands` rows under a per-user advisory lock;
  - WP-104: the four test flags are floored at `ask` always, and `.git` is not repository content;
  - WP-105: a resume from a pause at Ready always re-enters `rebase_gate`; a missing shadow base is refused, never substituted;
  - WP-106: a malformed stored project setting refuses the run by name at admission; `APP_JOBS_SCHEMA` accepts only `pgboss`;
  - WP-109: one shared in-place append retry; an older queued proposal for the same path is discarded with a reason;
  - WP-110: the GitLab merge-request poller is built, not written out of technical/06;
  - WP-111: resolve-on-merge is opt-in per binding, and comment and link stay refused (Q43);
  - WP-112: the task export is served with an event cap, and the run's settings snapshot is read at `transcript.read`;
  - WP-113: **Q111** is implemented per its recommendation (c);
  - WP-114: removing an integration **retires** it — its secrets destroyed, its row kept for the audit's foreign key.

| WP | Title | Depends on | Acceptance criteria |
|---|---|---|---|
| WP-101 | **A cancel ends the record while the session keeps spending, and N API processes admit N paid steers.** Folds PROGRESS backlog **294**: `run_commands.kind` admits only `steer` and `take_over`, so a cancel of a live run never reaches the session, which runs to its own end and is charged late. Folds **295**'s steer half: the one-per-five-seconds window is a per-process `Map`, and since WP-85 every admitted steer is a paid turn. **Built per TD-028 decision 11** (M6 amendment): a cancel of a run whose lease is live records a `cancel` row in the aggregate operation's transaction (the task is paused there, as today) and answers `202`; the holder applies it as `handle.stop`, so the run ends `cancelled` in its own process with its measured cost, which is the take-over's shape. A run with no live lease (absent or expired) is ended in place as today. **Ruling (295):** the window is shared state — admitting a steer takes `pg_advisory_xact_lock` on the user and reads that user's `steer` rows inside the interval; no new table. 295's webhook-bucket half stays deferred until a vendor's measured burst is reported | WP-85, WP-72, WP-47 | **(1)** A new forward-only migration admits `cancel` in `run_commands.kind`; migration 0060 is not edited. **(2)** On WP-72's two-process topology (`test/e2e/topology/`), a cancel answered by `app` stops the fake session running in `runner` before its script ends; the row reads `applied`, the run `cancelled`, and the ledger carries its measured cost exactly once and not as `late`. **(3)** A cancel with no live lease ends the record in place, with a case each way (lease live, lease expired). A holder that dies before applying leaves the row to the lease sweep, which closes it `run_ended`, asserted. **(4)** A steer still pending when the cancel lands is closed `run_ended` by the run's own ending, asserted. **(5)** Two steers by one user inside five seconds, one through each of two `ROLE=api` processes, admit exactly one; the second gets the window's existing refusal; a canary without the lock fails by name. **(6)** The route description, the command's docblock, `docs/user-guide.md`'s cancel paragraph and its *not built* row, technical/08's cancel and steer rows and technical/05 § 2 say which branch happens, and the Q52 sentences go (rule 83) |
| WP-102 | **Every task that edits an existing test pays a second code review and business review.** Implements **Q109** as **(b)**, the architect's M6 choice (reversible by the founder), amending WP-81's ruling that the tamper check lives entirely inside `ci_gate`'s read. **Ruling:** `ci_gate` still passes a declared-but-unjudged protected path provisionally (`protected_paths_awaiting_review`), now records `tasks.ci_head_sha` as for any pass, and records the provisionally excused paths on the task (a narrow column whose one writer is the CI settlement). The rebase gate's settlement compares them with the latest Review Verdict's confirmations **in its own transaction, with no provider call**. Confirmed: the settlement proceeds to Ready. Not confirmed: the task returns to implementation with the tamper reason and spends `ci_fix`, which is the return `ci_gate` would have made. A head that moved after the CI read still re-enters `ci_gate` through WP-79's existing path, unchanged | WP-81, WP-79, WP-99 | **(1)** *Measure first*: on the fake-Claude e2e, a task whose plan declares a change to an existing test counts its runs per stage before the change (expected: one extra `code_review` and one extra `business_review`); the numbers go into the WP notes. **(2)** After the change the same task reaches Ready with one run of each review stage, asserted by count. **(3)** A declared path the review did not confirm returns the task to implementation with the tamper reason and spends one `ci_fix`; a canary that skips the comparison fails by name. **(4)** An undeclared change is still returned by `ci_gate` on the first pass, asserted. **(5)** A new migration for the column; technical/02's gate description, technical/04's WP-81 amendment and Q109 say where the second half of the check now lives (rule 83), and the Checks panel's tamper item reads the settlement's outcome |
| WP-103 | **A run container whose create answer was lost has no owner.** Folds PROGRESS backlog **286**, with **136**'s question about a production producer. **Built per TD-028 decision 12** (M6 amendment): the launcher gains one authenticated read verb that answers the run ids of the containers it labelled, read from the daemon rather than its memory, and `destroy` by run id works for a container it holds no handle for (found by label). The reaper is a **runner-side** recovery row, because the runner reads `runs` and the launcher still reads no database (TD-021): a labelled run id whose run is terminal, or unknown and older than a stated grace, is destroyed, one attempt per id per pass, and counted. The launcher abandons a create whose request closed before the response and removes what it made, or its docblock says why it cannot | WP-53, WP-77, WP-82, WP-86 | **(1)** *Needs measurement* (a daemon, rule 66), extending `scripts/launcher-control-plane-check.mjs` as WP-82 did: (a) a launcher restart during a create — which objects exist afterwards and whether a container runs; (b) a create that outlives the client's timeout with the launcher alive — whether it completes, and how many containers a task accumulates over its three start attempts; (c) whether an unattached shim ever exits. The numbers go into 286. **(2)** An orphan for a terminal run is removed with its sidecar, network and control directory; a live run's container is kept (the negative); an unknown run id older than the grace is removed. **(3)** Each removal is a log line naming the run id and a counted metric, and WP-86's `run_alive` count moves as expected before and after. **(4)** The new verb is authenticated and idempotent like the other control-plane operations (decisions 3 and 4), with a protocol case and a client case. **(5)** The labels carry no instance identity today: either the row adds one so a second instance on the same daemon is never reaped, or the residual is stated at the verb. **(6)** technical/05's WP-53 amendment, `apps/launcher/src/control-plane.ts`'s docblock, TD-028's second WP-53 amendment and `CLAUDE.md`'s WP-53 paragraph state the bound (rule 83) |
| WP-104 | **What a run can write around its policy.** Folds PROGRESS backlog **281** (four allowed test spellings write or delete a protected path with no `ask`), **282** (`.git/` is neither protected nor flagged, and a `core.fsmonitor` written there turns the read-only `git status` into an arbitrary command), **283** (a write through a tracked directory symlink into a protected tree matches no pattern), **277** (the write-content guard does not apply the minted-credential shapes) and **285** (`CLAUDE_CODE_DISABLE_NONESSENTIAL_TRAFFIC` is set on the container and never reaches the CLI). **Rulings:** 281 — `HAZARDOUS_ARGUMENTS` floors the four flags at `ask` **always**, in every spelling the CLIs accept, with the over-block stated at the entry; 282 — the guard denies any path with a folded `.git` segment, because git's configuration is not repository content, and the run container's git gets `core.fsmonitor=false` through the reserved `GIT_CONFIG_*` environment; 283 — a write at or under an `opaque` listing entry is judged as a write to an existing protected path; 277 — per TD-012's M6 amendment, `guardWriteContent` uses the redactor's corpus; 285 — `platformEnvironment` carries the variable | WP-99, WP-80, WP-82 | **(1)** One `command-policy.test.ts` case per spelling (`--basetemp=x` and `--basetemp x`, `--junitxml` and `--junit-xml`, `go test -c -o` and `-o=`, `cargo test --target-dir` and `--target-dir=`) reads `ask`; a canary removing the floor fails by name; the docblock stops calling them contained. **(2)** `.git/config`, `.GIT/config` under the fold and `sub/.git/hooks/x` are denied with the reason. *Needs measurement* (a daemon): a `core.fsmonitor` a run writes fires under `git status` in `platform-runtime` before the change and not after. **(3)** `link/conftest.py` with `link` opaque is denied; a canary restoring the pattern-first order fails by name. **(4)** With a custom-prefix shape installed, a write carrying a value of that shape is denied under `minted-credential-shape`, with a canary on the default argument. **(5)** An `options.test.ts` case asserts the variable is in the spawn environment and survives a `RunSpec.env` that sets it to `0`; the container-environment comment is rewritten. **(6)** technical/04's path-guard rows and WP-99 amendment, technical/05's egress paragraph and backlog 137's text follow (rule 83) |
| WP-118 | **The run container's environment never reaches the `claude` process.** Folds PROGRESS backlog **342** (major): the egress proxy (`HTTPS_PROXY`/`HTTP_PROXY`/`NO_PROXY`), `HOME`, `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` and the credential helper's `GIT_CONFIG_*` are set only on the run container, `PATH` is set nowhere, and the shim execs the CLI with the spawn frame's environment alone, so on the `internal: true` run network the CLI has no route to `api.anthropic.com` (read off the tree; the consequence is inferred, not measured). **Built per TD-025's amendment (backlog 342, session 10)**: the launcher answers the container-derived, non-secret values on `ProvisionedRunWorkspace` beside `claudeCodePath`, as a strict structured field (never a free-form record), produced by the one launcher function that also writes the container's environment; the runner composes the CLI's whole environment in one function (the model credential, those values, `platformEnvironment`, and **one** `GIT_CONFIG_*` list numbered once, so `credential.helper` and WP-104's `core.fsmonitor` are `GIT_CONFIG_COUNT=2`); the shim is unchanged and `env` still replaces. **Placed immediately after WP-104** because it blocks every containerised production run; **before WP-105** (both write `packages/infrastructure/src/workspace/provider.ts`); **WP-33 depends on it** (its first real-credential run would fail on it) | WP-104, WP-82, WP-53 | **(1)** *Measure first* (a daemon, no model credential; rules 27 and 66): extend `scripts/launcher-control-plane-check.mjs` so `fake-claude-cli` reports the **names** in its own `/proc/self/environ`, never values, except `GIT_CONFIG_COUNT` and each `GIT_CONFIG_KEY_<n>` (configuration names, not secrets). Record the result on today's tree in 342. In the same pass, start the image's real `claude` through the same path with an obviously fake API key and record the sidecar's log for `api.anthropic.com` and the CLI's own error, which settles consequence (a) either way. If the names are already present, 342 closes as not-a-defect with the measurement and the row stops here. **(2)** After the fix the check asserts: `HTTPS_PROXY`, `HTTP_PROXY` and `NO_PROXY` when the run has a sidecar, and `HOME`, `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` and `PATH` always; no `RUNLET_*` name; `GIT_CONFIG_COUNT=2` with `credential.helper` and `core.fsmonitor`; and the real-CLI leg's `CONNECT api.anthropic.com` reaching the sidecar. The recording from (1) is the canary: the check fails on the pre-fix tree. **(3)** The answer's new field is a strict zod schema in `packages/infrastructure/src/launcher/protocol.ts`, with protocol cases refusing an unknown key, a free-form entry and a `RUNLET_`-named variable. `PATH` is the run image's own declared `Config.Env` value, read where `claudeCodePath` is verified, and an image that declares none is `invalid_spec` naming the image. A unit case asserts that the container's environment and the answer come from the one function, with a canary that edits one side. **(4)** `options.test.ts` cases: the git list carries both keys under `COUNT=2` with contiguous indices; a configuration key present on both sides is refused by name, never silently deduplicated; `RunSpec.env` cannot override a launcher-answered name or any `GIT_CONFIG_*`; with no launcher answer (no provisioner) the environment is today's, and the case says so. **(5)** No new secret crosses the socket: a unit case runs every answered value through the TD-012 detector, and the frame's only secret stays the model credential it already carries. A case asserts that no log line of `spawn-adapter.ts` or `shim.ts` carries an `env` value. **(6)** Rule 83: the `RESERVED_ENV_PREFIXES` docblock and the container-environment comment in `provider.ts`, technical/04's `env` row (where each value comes from), technical/05's sidecar sentence, backlog 285's cost text, `docs/research/12-run-shim-verification.md`, `docs/TODO.md`'s backlog 137 line (a) and `CLAUDE.md`'s WP-53 paragraph follow |
| WP-105 | **A judgement that reads as current when it is stale, absent, or taken on another base.** Folds PROGRESS backlog **274** (a task paused at Ready resumes on a rebase judgement older than its target branch), **280** (a `ci_gate` row written before WP-81 reads *clean* on the tamper item), **289** (`get_task_context` answers earlier verdicts with nothing tying a verdict to a return), **303** (`reviewedMergeRequestPaths` absorbs `TransactionOpenError`), and WP-98's first discovered-work item: a shadow task whose base commit is missing from the mirror checks out a new branch *named* after the sha at the default branch's head, so the run starts on today's tree, which Q82 (a) says must be refused (numbered by the refiner with WP-98's ledger row). **Rulings:** 274 — option (c): a resume from a pause at Ready always re-enters `rebase_gate`, at the cost of one mergeability read and no loop (WP-79's `enter`); 280 — the settlement's outcome is recorded on the stage row, and a row without it reads *not checked (before WP-81)*; 289 — option (b): the `feedback` section carries each return's `cause`, and the tool keeps the history; 303 — rethrow; the shadow base — refused, never substituted | WP-79, WP-81, WP-83, WP-76, WP-102, WP-104 | **(1)** A resume from a pause at Ready re-enters `rebase_gate`, a conflict found there raises WP-26's warning, and no loop is spent, asserted in the e2e tier. **(2)** `test:ui` cases each way for the tamper item (a migration only if the outcome column's constraint needs one). **(3)** After a review return and then a CI return, the tool's `feedback` names which verdict each return was for, with a unit case. **(4)** A unit case in `review-paths` inside a marked transaction asserts the throw, with a canary that absorbs it. **(5)** A shadow task whose base commit is absent from the mirror fails its start by name and escalates as Q59a says; it never runs on the default branch (a workspace case, and an e2e case if the fake git can withhold a commit). **(6)** `ready-head.ts`'s docblock gap, technical/04 beside WP-83's sentence and Q82 follow (rule 83) |
| WP-106 | **Configuration read without its schema.** Folds PROGRESS backlog **311**'s project half (the settings port and the knowledge read cast `projects.config`, so a value the screen refuses reaches admission, the planner and the frozen snapshot) and **296** (`APP_JOBS_SCHEMA` is read by the server and by nothing `migrate` runs). **Rulings:** 311 — both reads parse with `agenticConfigSchema`, and a failure refuses the run **by name at admission** (key paths, redacted and bounded values, the `PUT` that fixes it), never read as empty, because an empty layer drops restrictions somebody wrote (rule 20); `contextBudgetRefusal`'s bespoke refusal is folded into it. 296 — option (b): a process that finds `APP_JOBS_SCHEMA` set to anything but `pgboss` refuses to start and names the variable and `migrate`, and `.env.example` says the value is fixed | WP-91, WP-93, WP-86 | **(1)** *Measure first*: a census of every key the run path reads (WIP limits, commands, protected paths, reviewers, status mapping, budgets, context budget) and the direction each fails under a malformed stored value (open, closed or throws), pasted into 311. **(2)** A unit case per read with an out-of-schema `pipeline.wip` refuses by name; a canary restoring the cast fails. **(3)** The knowledge read refuses the same way, and the index job records the refusal instead of proceeding. **(4)** `APP_JOBS_SCHEMA=jobs` refuses at startup naming both; `pgboss` or unset starts. `.env.example`, the docblock of `packages/infrastructure/src/jobs/config.ts` and the compose-config assertion follow. **(5)** technical/12 states both (rule 83) |
| WP-107 | **A credential one process knows and another does not.** Folds PROGRESS backlog **276** (a custom-prefix shape reaches other processes only at their next refresh, up to 5 s later, and a refresher that keeps failing keeps a stale set with a `warn`), **316** (a binding credential committed to `.agentic/prompts/` is stored in the reading, sent to the model and kept in `runs.user_prompt`) and **278** (an account's load failure says *binding*, and the mint refusal names GitLab's `token_prefix` from the application ring). **Built per TD-012's M6 amendment**: the shape write notifies on commit through the transactional broadcast, which every `ROLE` subscribes on its own channel, and the timer stays as the guarantee; after a stated number of consecutive failed refreshes the process reports at `error`; the repository reading redacts prompt texts with an exact-value redactor over the decrypted credentials of the project's bindings before it stores them | WP-80, WP-85, WP-92, WP-106 | **(1)** An integration case: a second pool holds a new shape rule before the timer would fire. A check holds that the refresh and the listener are installed for **every** `ROLE` (the residual the orchestrator added to 276). **(2)** Consecutive failed refreshes past the bound log once at `error`, with a case. **(3)** A unit case plants a binding credential no pattern rule knows in a prompt file and asserts it is absent from the stored reading and from `runs.user_prompt`, with a canary. **(4)** The account path says *integration*, and both hints come from a provider-declared string on the registration; unit cases pin both texts. **(5)** `apps/server/src/knowledge.ts`'s stale comment and technical/05's WP-80 amendment and redaction section follow (rule 83) |
| WP-108 | **Three things that wait for ever or never fire.** Folds PROGRESS backlog **291** (a reminder whose arm was lost is never recovered, and rows open at WP-84's deploy got none), **320** (a discovery task whose `stage.execute` enqueue was lost stays `active` for ever and blocks every re-evaluation) and **325** (a pg-boss job that spent its retries is invisible). **Rulings:** 291 — a fourth row of `recovery/deadline.ts`'s table; a backfilled row whose reminder time has passed and whose deadline has not is reminded on the next pass, because one reminder before the escalation is BD-006's purpose; 320 — a row of `recovery/stranded.ts`'s table, re-enqueued once under a mark and then escalated with a brief; 325 — measure, then decide | WP-84, WP-94, WP-36, WP-95, WP-103 | **(1)** A lost reminder arm is found and reminded once; a row past its deadline is not; a canary with the row removed fails by name; `reminders.ts`'s residual and technical/02's reminder line are corrected. **(2)** *Measure first*: every `enqueueStage` call site is read for the same after-commit loss, and the row covers the class if more than discovery is exposed. A recovery e2e drops the discovery enqueue, and the task then runs once or escalates. The rediscovery gate's *active* sentence says how long ago the task started and names cancel. **(3)** *Measure first*: a census of registered job names, each marked *bounds and escalates itself* or *relies on pg-boss retries* with its `retryLimit`, pasted into 325. Then every job of the second kind whose loss drops a task or a provider write gets `stage.execute`'s bound-and-escalate shape, **or** an admin read of pg-boss's failed jobs is added beside the dead-letter list (name, attempts, last error redacted and bounded, never the payload); the row states which and why. A census test holds the classification for any new job name |
| WP-109 | **A knowledge pass that loses a race waits a minute, and a re-evaluation queues the same page twice.** Folds PROGRESS backlog **333** (four knowledge writers read the stream sequence outside their transaction and never retry a `StreamConflictError`) and **319** (the curator dedupes against the index, not the queue). **Rulings:** 333 — one shared `appendOnProjectWithRetry` beside `delivery-measures.ts`'s `appendOnProject` (bound 4; a provider call made before the append is never repeated), used by the four writers and by `delivery-measures.ts`; 319 — option (a): the recorder marks an older **queued** proposal for the same path `discarded`, with a platform reason naming the newer row's task | WP-90, WP-94, WP-18b | **(1)** Per writer, `StreamConflictError` injected on three appends still lands the pass, with the apply pass's provider calls counted once; on four the job throws. **(2)** `test/e2e/pipeline/librarian.e2e.test.ts` drops `holdUntilMeasured` and stays green. *Needs measurement*: that test at load ≈ 12, before and after, with the numbers in 333. **(3)** Two discovery records that draft one page leave one pending proposal and one `discarded` with its reason; a canary with the queue read disarmed. **(4)** 319's apply-side hypothesis (two knowledge merge requests creating one file) is measured against the fake git provider or ruled out |
| WP-110 | **A GitLab binding with no public URL hears nothing about its merge requests, and a status-rule poll misses edits.** Folds PROGRESS backlog **297** (technical/06 specifies a merge-request poller beside the ticket one, and WP-87 built tickets only) and **298** (a poll asks only for the pick-up rule, so a ticket the status mapping moved on is never re-read). **Rulings:** 297 — option (a): a per-binding `mr.poll` job on WP-87's shape (a cursor on the binding, each listed merge request recorded through `recordNormalisedDelivery` on the same inbox key rule, a recovery sweep); 298 — each ticket poll also reads the tickets of the binding's live tasks changed since the cursor and records `ticket.updated` only, never `ticket.matched` | WP-87, WP-60, WP-24 | **(1)** A new migration for the merge-request cursor; the job is on `JOB_QUEUE_DEFINITIONS` and its census. The GitLab listing fixtures carry `source` blocks and `SOURCES.md` names their pages. **(2)** An integration case: a poll emits `mr.opened`, a second poll appends nothing, and a webhook delivery of the same event is deduplicated. **(3)** A review-only e2e started by a poll, and a task that learns its merge request merged from a poll. **(4)** A status-rule binding, a task at a later status, an edit and one poll: the ticket signal is stamped. The extra read's bound is stated as a number. **(5)** The GitLab setup guide, `docs/operator-guide.md` and technical/06's residual sentences are rewritten (rule 83) |
| WP-111 | **Two provider writes: one repeats, one never happens.** Folds PROGRESS backlog **288** (the Jira marker search reads only the oldest hundred comments, so an affected ticket gets a new workpad on every stage transition) and **302**'s capability half (product/08's on-merge Sentry resolve is unbuilt; the false sentences were corrected at `6384689`). **Rulings:** 288 — the search pages by `startAt` to `total` under a stated page bound, and a search that hits the bound **fails** (rule 20) instead of answering `null`, because `null` is the answer that posts; 302 — option (b), opt-in: a binding flag `resolve_on_merge`, off by default because product/08 says *"optional"*, enables a `pipeline.outbound` duty on `mr.merged` for a bug task: `linkedIssues` over the ticket snapshot, then the adapter's `resolve` through `observabilityForProject` and the executor. `comment` and `linkMergeRequest` stay refused (Q43) | WP-89, WP-83, WP-110 | **(1)** A Jira replay with 150 comments and the workpad at position 120 finds and PUTs it; hitting the bound fails by name; a canary restoring the single page fails. **(2)** An e2e: a merge yields one `integration_actions` row per linked issue on a flagged binding, none on an unflagged one and none for a project with no Sentry binding; a replayed `mr.merged` resolves nothing twice. **(3)** The Sentry setup guide's § 5 and the port docblock describe what happens when the flag is set, and product/08:52's comment half goes to the orchestrator as exact text (rule 83) |
| WP-112 | **The run and task record product/09 calls exportable.** Folds PROGRESS backlog **310** (`GET /api/tasks/:id/export` is listed, typed and permissioned, and nothing serves it), **309** (the run record publishes neither `settings_hash` nor the snapshot WP-91 writes) and **293** (`reminders_sent` is published as a bare integer that counts raised reminders). **Rulings:** 310 — option (a): served at `task.export`, scoped to the task's project, one JSON document built from the existing read projections — the task and its stages, questions, approvals, `human_actions` rows, artifact metadata, run records, and its events capped at a stated count with `truncated`; transcripts stay on their own download. 309 — `runRecordSchema.settings_hash` (null means created before WP-91) and `GET /api/runs/:id/settings` at `transcript.read`, because the snapshot can carry operator free text, with a typed 409 for a pre-WP-91 row as `/prompt` has. 293 — a docblock on the published field | WP-91, WP-84, WP-15h, WP-101 | **(1)** The export through the real router: 401, a wrong-project 403, a 200 whose body parses with the extended `taskExportResponseSchema`, and the event cap's `truncated` asserted; the census gains a hand assertion as `kb/health` has. **(2)** The run screen shows the hash and marks a change from the task's previous run (`test:ui`); `/settings` answers 200 and the 409 through the real router. **(3)** `pnpm schemas` is regenerated, and the client census gains the settings call in both directions. **(4)** technical/08's task and run rows and product/09's export line are true as written (rule 83) |
| WP-113 | **Settings nobody can see until a run has spent money.** Folds PROGRESS backlog **315** (the reading's prompt half has no read surface, and nothing says an edit applies at the next reading) and **318** (lowering an organisation maximum does not say which projects it now caps). Implements **Q111** per its recommendation **(c)** (reversible by the founder): one digest line when a project's maintenance pause at Observe begins, one when it ends, and nothing between. **Rulings:** 315 — (a) to (c) of the entry's *Done*; product/13:118's *diff against defaults* is out of this row; 318 — `PATCH /api/org`'s response carries `capped_projects` (project, key, before, after), computed in the write's transaction; Q111 — the maintenance pass records each project's last blocker (a narrow column) and turns a transition into a report | WP-92, WP-93, WP-94, WP-106, WP-65 | **(1)** `repositoryConfigReadingSchema` gains the per-file prompt half (path, status, pre-cut length, whether the 8 000-character cut applies, never the text) and the effective view gains the per-stage resolution; schemas are regenerated. **(2)** The settings screen shows both, with the reading's commit and a *Re-read now* action on the existing refresh, and a `test:ui` case. **(3)** The user guide gains the paragraph: where the files live, the default names, the cut, and that an edit applies at the next index run or refresh, not at the merge. **(4)** A route unit case: lowering the autonomy maximum lists the capped projects with before and after; raising it lists none. **(5)** Unit cases for Q111: a pause beginning gives one line, no change gives none, the end gives one; product/18's digest sentence and Q111's record follow (rule 83) |
| WP-114 | **An integration cannot be rotated or retired, and two operator screens stop short.** Folds PROGRESS backlog **331** (no product path re-seals a credential or removes an integration), **332** (the forms offer a provider's required fields only), **324** (the dead-letter screen shows the newest 50 and stops) and **327** (every dead-letter read error is named a permission problem). **Rulings:** 331 — two commands. `POST /api/integrations/:id/secrets` re-seals `secret_refs` (field → environment-variable name, the `APP_INTEGRATION_SECRET_ENV` allow-list, `Idempotency-Key`, a `human_actions` row, `health` reset). `DELETE /api/integrations/:id` **retires** the integration: it destroys the integration's `secrets` rows and sets `retired_at` (a migration), and keeps the row, because `integration_actions.integration_id` is a `NOT NULL` foreign key and the audit must keep naming the credential a call used (BD-003). **What M6 deliberately does not contain.**
- `.agentic/pipeline.yml` and `pipeline.custom_stages` (Q56), which stay parsed, unread and reported `not_applied`.
- Business review after an Assist hand-back (**Q106**), scoping-only for a template with no architecture stage (**Q99**) and the storage panel (**Q108**). All three are the founder's.
- A second provider of any type, phase-2 search, universal-ctags (**Q57**), and the git port's *"which account am I"* member (backlog **266**, still declined: no new evidence).
- Sentry comment and merge-request link (**Q43**: no documented endpoint), and a per-binding Sentry project filter (backlog **305**, a stated residual nobody has asked to narrow).
- A configurable webhook rate limit (backlog **295**'s second half), until a vendor's measured burst is reported.
- A held quiet-hours question posted with buttons when the window closes (backlog **301**): that is a product/18 change; M6 only documents the behaviour.
- product/13:118's *diff against defaults* (backlog **315**'s larger half), which needs a notion of the shipped default per key.
- Pushing a newer configuration onto an open export branch (**313**), matching first-pass acceptance on the merge request (**308**), and R10/R13 parses beyond named paths (**321**).
- **No new product scope.** Every row closes a gap against an accepted document or implements a filed question's recommendation (Q109 (b), Q111 (c)).

## Milestone M7 — ready to dogfood

M6 made the record agree with the run. Its delivery filed fifty-five more entries (backlog 334–401), about three per row, and the backlog has not shrunk since M4. **M7 is written to converge, not to mirror.** Every entry graded major, minor or small whose consequence is real is scheduled: a lost signal, money, a credential, a false operator-facing statement, a product promise, or a CI that can hang. A nit is folded only where it rides with a row that already touches the same code. Every other entry gets an explicit disposition in § "Architect ruling (M7, session 11)" at the end of `docs/technical/PROGRESS.md`: **declined for 0.1** with the reason (its heading is then marked closed, so it leaves the open count) or **stays open, unscheduled** because it needs a human, a dogfood install or an external system. **What M7's completion means:** the open backlog is then only what this build cannot settle without a human (§ "What remains for 0.1" below). **A row names what it folds and does not restate the entry's evidence: read the entry first, then the row.** An open question is implemented per its recommendation and never waited on.

**No open entry is graded major or blocker.** The two M6 majors (**342**, **354**) were closed by WP-118 and WP-106. The M4–M6 test, every major has a row, holds with nothing to place.

**Twelve rows, WP-119…WP-130.** They fold **49** entries. **15** are declined for 0.1 and **8** stay open unscheduled; the table in PROGRESS.md has all 72 (71 numbered entries and backlog 295's deferred half).

**The order, and the argument for each group.**

1. **Money, fail-closed checks and credentials (WP-119…WP-121).** A run the platform stops for a stall or its wall clock is recorded as a measured zero and charged to no cap (**334**). A project command's path-writing flags and a template without a rebase gate walk past the protected-path rules (**343**, **338**). A prompt-file reading stored before WP-107, a credential left in `integrations.config` and an organisation account's token all still reach the model (**359**, **362**, **364**).
2. **Product promises (WP-122, WP-123).** product/04's manual "Start" from a ticket key has no route and no form, while the board tells users it exists (**379**). product/09's per-task export has no button (**381**). A poll-only GitLab binding has no review loop and no conflict re-check at Ready (**373**). Each is part of the MVP list in product/14.
3. **Work that fails, waits or collides with nothing to recover it (WP-124, WP-125).** A failed `knowledge.apply`, `onboarding.discovery` or human-awaited outbound duty is listed for an administrator and recovered by nothing (**366**). A burst drains at one job per poll interval (**392**). A knowledge proposal is lost to a settings refusal, or two merge requests create one file (**356**, **369**).
4. **Operations and instruments (WP-126…WP-129).** CI has no job timeout and once held `main` for 31 minutes (**401**). Two image checks share one compose network and can read another project's database (**347**). A run that cannot start, or that cannot reach the model, says nothing about why (**351**, **344**, **346**). The e2e fixture's cleanup removes a developer's live product run containers (**398**). Nothing holds the user guide to its screens (**400**) or `projects` to its writers (**383**).
5. **The documents last (WP-130).**

**Row sizing.** No row except the documents row folds more than five entries, and each row touches one cause or one module. A nit rides only where its code is already open. M5 and M6 rows took two to four hours of implementer time plus one to three review rounds; these are sized the same.

**Parallel-safe sets** (different packages, no shared files). First wave: `{WP-119, WP-120, WP-126}` (runner and cost path; domain policies; workflows and image-check scripts). Second wave: `{WP-121, WP-123, WP-124, WP-128}` (repository reading and loaders; the merge-request poll; recovery and jobs; the e2e harness). **Serialise** these pairs:
- WP-119 before WP-127: both write `packages/infrastructure/src/runner/claude-runner.ts` and `packages/application/src/pipeline/stage-executor.ts`.
- WP-121 before WP-122: both write `packages/contracts/src/api.ts` and regenerate `schemas/`.
- WP-124 before WP-125: both add rows to `recovery/stranded.ts`'s table.
- WP-122 before WP-129: WP-129's user-guide check reads the screens and the guide WP-122 changes.
- `docs/operator-guide.md` § 5 *Upgrade* gains one paragraph each from WP-121, WP-123, WP-126 and WP-127. Each is appended in merge order; none rewrites another's.
- WP-130 last: it must not edit a document another in-flight row edits.

WP-126 is parallel-safe with every row. It is the cheapest insurance for the rest, because a hung CI job holds `main` for six hours, so the orchestrator may start it first.

A row that adds a migration takes the next free number **at merge**, in merge order. An applied migration is never renumbered or edited (TD-011). Expected migrations: WP-121 (the reading's redaction mark and withheld list), WP-123 (the binding's last-seen default-branch head), and WP-125 only if its first criterion finds the merge request per path unrecorded.

**Needs measurement, never guessed.** Each of these is its row's criterion (1), taken before the fix is chosen (rule 27):
- the pytest `-o` and go flag forms (**343**, WP-120);
- which `pipeline.outbound` duties the next transition re-derives, and what a proposal reads after a failed apply (**366**, WP-124);
- the drain time at the shipped 2 s interval, and whether pg-boss's ready-count cache is populated with `persistQueueStats: false` (**392**, WP-124);
- whether the knowledge store records which merge request carries which path (**369**, WP-125);
- whether compose accepts `APP_PORT=0` on the pinned compose version (**348**, WP-126);
- the launcher's exit code and full log on a stop during a create, the pre-WP-103 orphan's object list, and the real CLI's retry and give-up with no route (**339**, **341**, **346**; a daemon, no model credential, WP-127);
- the command-API cancel wait under a delayed heartbeat and a dropped notify (**391**, WP-119);
- the e2e cleanup beside a disposable product instance (**398**, WP-128).

Still with WP-33 (a model credential): **137**'s CLI egress hosts, **173**'s divisor, and whether the real CLI writes an interrupted turn's `result` after a platform stop (**334**'s measurement half).

**Decisions taken before the rows**, so an implementer does not re-open them.
- **TD-012 amendment (M7)** (WP-121):
  - A reading records which redaction it was stored under. A pre-WP-107 reading is re-read once at upgrade. One that cannot be re-read serves no prompt text.
  - The exact-value set includes a provider's declared secret fields found in `integrations.config`, and the organisation communication accounts' credentials.
  - Withheld prompt texts are recorded on the reading.
- **TD-004 amendment (M7)** (WP-124): every queue's exhaustion has a declared shape, which is a recovery row, bound-and-escalate, or notification-shaped and listed only. There is no re-queue from the failed-jobs list in 0.1.
- **Decisions inside rows**:
  - WP-119: a platform stop reads the interrupted turn within the existing grace, and nothing read means unmeasured (`null`), never zero. `run.finished.cost` becomes nullable.
  - WP-120: a template with `rebase_gate` disabled and `ci_gate` enabled is refused.
  - WP-122: the manual start is `task.create` (member, already in the capability map). It bypasses the pick-up rule and nothing else.
  - WP-123: the poll's two new reads run only for a poll-only git integration, one with no webhook secret configured, so no delivery arrives by both paths. `mr.approved` stays webhook-only.
  - WP-124: `burstWhenReadyExceeds` or a shorter per-queue interval, never per-queue LISTEN/NOTIFY in 0.1.
  - WP-125: a second `create` of a path that an unmerged knowledge merge request already carries is deferred, never stacked.
  - WP-126: the compose default network is project-scoped.
  - WP-127: `api_retry` is not progress for the stall detector, and a start failure's escalation carries a closed-vocabulary cause.
  - WP-128: the harness removes only what its own launcher instance created.
  - WP-129: `projects` gets a census and no `version` column.
- **Filed:** **Q112** (whether a member's task export carries the audit half; built as *no*, per the recommendation).

| WP-119 | **A run the platform stops is recorded as free, and a cancel's wait is unexplained.** Folds PROGRESS backlog **334** (a `stalled` or `timed_out` run's outcome carries `usd 0` and no usage, `runs.finish` writes it as measured, the ledger takes `no_spend`; and `run.finished.cost` states `{usd: 0}` for every stop that measured nothing). Also folds **336** (a stop refused `register_miss` while this process still leases the run leaves the session running unseen), **391** (the command-API e2e's cancel once never reached its ending and charge within 92 s) and **396** (the asking scenario exists four times; it rides with 391's file). **Rulings:** 334 — `stalled` and `timed_out` join the human stops in reading the interrupted turn's `result` within the existing `INTERRUPT_GRACE_MS`. The grace is the bounded price, and the wall-clock stop is the most expensive run the platform ends. Where nothing is read, the outcome's cost is **unmeasured**: the run row's cost columns are `null`, there is no ledger row, and the event carries `null`. `runFinishedEvent.cost` becomes `runCostSchema.nullish()` as `run.failed`'s already is, and the cost ledger treats `null` as *no entry*, as the lease sweep's path does.
