**What M7 deliberately does not contain.**
- `.agentic/pipeline.yml` and `pipeline.custom_stages` (**Q56**), and a reader of `pipeline.template_overrides.*.stages.*.enabled` (backlog 220's reader, which waits on **Q99**). WP-120 only makes it safe to add later.
- The founder's questions: **Q99**, **Q106**, **Q108** (the storage panel, backlog **232**), **Q34**'s veto, and **Q112**'s confirmation.
- A second provider of any type, phase-2 search, universal-ctags (**Q57**), the git port's *"which account am I"* (**266**), Sentry comment and link (**Q43**), and a per-binding Sentry project filter (**305**).
- A re-queue button on the failed-jobs list (TD-004's M7 amendment), a configurable webhook rate limit (**295**'s second half), and `mr.approved` from a poll.
- A project's own container variables (**345**, behind Q62), the platform's own ticket writes recognised as such (**374**), a loop-or-actor return cause (**353**), and a host identity in the harness marker (**397**).
- Anything that needs a model credential (WP-33), a Jira Cloud site (**375**, 377's and 288's recordings), a real vault (**171**) or a real batch (**238**).
- **No new product scope.** Every row closes a gap against an accepted document (product/04, product/09, product/14's MVP list) or a filed entry.

**What remains for 0.1 once M7 is DONE.** v0.1's scope (M1–M3) is built, and M4–M7 hardened it. What is left needs a human, and the build cannot settle it:
1. **WP-33's model credential.** The role-prompt evals (blocker brief 1), and the real-CLI measurements filed against it (**137**'s egress hosts, **173**'s divisor, **334**'s interrupted result).
2. **WP-78's upstream StrykerJS release** (blocker brief 2).
3. **product/14's exit criteria**, which only dogfooding can meet: 20 real tickets across the two founder projects, ≥ 60 % merged, cost per merged feature, ≥ 10 accepted KB proposals, zero secrets incidents, a stranger's install in under an hour, shadow mode on 10 closed tickets per project, and review-only on ≥ 20 human merge requests. The entries waiting on that install are **171**, **238**, **375** and **132**'s next occurrence.
4. **The founder's decisions:** **Q99**, **Q106**, **Q108**, **Q34**, **Q112**, and the confirmations listed in the ruling. The `RELEASE_VERSIONING` switch is still unset; Q96 (4) recommends waiting for WP-33's credential.

**Convergence rule for M7's own findings.** A finding inside an M7 row's cause is closed in that row's review rounds. A new entry graded major gets a row. Every other entry filed during M7 waits for the first architect pass after dogfooding starts, so that evidence from real use, not the backlog's length, decides what comes after 0.1.

## Milestone M8 — the first local test (user-directed, session 11)

**Why this milestone exists, against M7's convergence rule.** On 2026-10-03 the product owner set a date:
in 24 hours they start testing the platform themselves, on their own projects (Autix: **GitLab** and
**Jira Cloud**), in BD-004's **`local`** provider mode (a Claude Code subscription token from
`claude setup-token` in their own `.env`, `docker compose -f compose.yml -f compose.local.yml up`), and
asked for as much as can be delivered before then. That is the dogfooding M7's rule waits for, started
by the person the rule defers to, so the minors that stand between that test and a clean first day are
scheduled now. **No model credential is used by any row**: the product owner declined it, so every real-CLI
measurement is taken with a bogus token, and the first real model run is the product owner's.
The answers given with that instruction: Q34, Q99 and Q106 accepted as implemented (recorded by WP-135).

**Order and machine.** WP-132 and WP-133 hold Docker and run one at a time; WP-134 holds none; WP-135 is
last. **WP-138, WP-137 and WP-136 were added by the architect on 2026-10-03** (session 11's M8 additions)
after WP-135 found the CI gate's poll-only bound and the orchestrator found that nothing opens a code merge
request; their order is the table's — WP-138 first (nothing reaches a merge request without it), then
WP-137 (without it no stage runs on gitlab.com Free), then WP-136. None needs Docker except WP-137's
provisioner case, if it chooses the launcher check. Autix is PHP + Composer and its CI covers it, so the
run image is **not** changed: agents edit, and the pipeline is the evidence. A row that cannot finish inside the product owner's window is recorded as such and the next row starts.

**Round 3 (architect, 2026-10-04, the founder's answers of that date; PROGRESS § "Architect ruling (M8 round 3, session 11)").** WP-140…WP-146 in the table's order, which is their value to the test: the pre-flight first (it needs no decision and tells the product owner whether `local` mode authenticates), then the seat-free credential, the default-branch move the product owner is about to make, the CI warning, the stop that loses a run, Jira moves, and the SSH deploy key last — the largest, and the one that may land after the test starts. The M8 preamble's *no model credential is used by any row* is superseded for **WP-140 only**, by the founder's answer. WP-140 and WP-144's real-daemon leg hold Docker; WP-146's does too; none of the others does.

## Milestone M9 — what the first local test found (architect, session 12)

**Why this milestone exists.** M8 is complete (WP-132…WP-149). The product owner's first local test
on Autix (gitlab.com Free, poll-only, Jira Cloud, `local` mode) has run since 2026-10-05 and filed
PROGRESS backlog **450–497**. The product owner's own sessions fixed most of it in place (the headings
marked **RESOLVED**). M9 schedules what is still open, in the protocol's order: what holds money
wrongly first, then what a person meets on every task (the run's own boundary, an escalated task's
ways forward, the command scanner, the task page), then onboarding, then what fails without telling
anyone, then the rest. The disposition of every open backlog heading is PROGRESS § "Architect ruling
(M9, session 12)". **No row needs a model credential, a GitLab or a Jira**: every criterion is met with
fakes, the Testcontainers database, and (named where it applies) the Docker daemon. WP-33 stays
BLOCKED and is not a row. **Migrations** are numbered in the order the rows land: the first row that
needs one takes **0085**, the next **0086**, and so on; the numbers below assume table order.

| WP | Title | Deps | Acceptance criteria |
|---|---|---|---|
| WP-150 | **A run that never reached the CLI holds no money** (backlog **410**, **489**'s money half; **465** per **Q116 (a)**). BD-010's 2026-10-06 amendment. **Migration: yes (0085).** **Rulings:** **(a)** `runs.cli_spawn_requested_at timestamptz`, **backfilled to `started_at` for every existing row**, so a run that ended before the column existed is treated as having reached the CLI and nothing held today is released by the migration. **(b)** It is written by a narrow compare-and-set repository method (only where it is null and the run has not ended), in **its own committed transaction**, by the process holding the run, **after** the shim's `hello.ok` and **before** the `spawn` frame is sent (`packages/infrastructure/src/runlet/spawn-adapter.ts:308-335` today sends it from inside the `hello.ok` case). The adapter stays free of the database: the runner composes an awaited `beforeSpawn` callback. A failed or refused write sends no `spawn` — no CLI without the marker. The ask executor's runs get the same. **(c)** A run whose marker is null is a **measured zero**: its ending writes the zero the start-failure path writes (`recordUnstarted`'s `NO_COST`, `packages/application/src/pipeline/stage-executor.ts:2178`, `:2303`), and the pending-spend reads stop holding it (`packages/infrastructure/src/cost/pending-run-spend.ts:66-68` and `:94-95`, the task cap's read at `packages/infrastructure/src/pipeline/postgres-pipeline-store.ts:1794-1797`). **(d)** Every way a run ends before its marker — the shim refused the handshake, a connect timeout before `hello.ok`, the marker write refused, a cancel ended in place, a lease sweep during provisioning — ends through the **start-failure** path with WP-127's closed-vocabulary cause naming which step it reached, never as `crash` (today `packages/infrastructure/src/runner/claude-runner.ts:997-1015`). A run with the marker and no figure keeps WP-131's hold, unchanged. ***Amended 2026-10-06*** (PROGRESS § "Architect ruling (M9, session 12)", amendment): *as built*, the start-failure path is for the runner's own pre-marker endings. **A cancel before the marker keeps `cancelled`**: a person ended it, the task is paused, not escalated or retried, and WP-127's vocabulary names workspace failures, not human decisions. **A sweep keeps `failed` / `lease_expired`** and carries the start-failure shape (`ended_before_cli_spawn`) on `exit_detail` and `run.failed`. Both are measured zeros. The forms are: `${y[x]}`, `${#y[x]}`, `${z@P}` (and every other `${name@op}`), `${!x}`, `${z:x}` and `${z:0:x}`, `$[x]`, `((x))`, `let x`, `declare -i`/`typeset -i`/`local -i`/`export -i`, `[[ x -eq 1 ]]` (and `-ne`, `-lt`, `-le`, `-gt`, `-ge`), and an indexed-array assignment `y[x]=1`. Bash 5.2 and dash (the run image's shells) record which of them run the payload. The backlog's expected verdict (`allow` / `unattended_auto` under `auto`, `unattended_deny` under `deny`) and its open question (whether a bare `x=$(cat f)` is ever `allow`) are answered in the row notes. **(b)** **Fail closed.** A new `UNCERTAINTY` entry is worded for the model: *write the value literally; the platform cannot read an expansion that evaluates a variable's text*. It fires on every form in (a) that the bash manual's *Arithmetic Evaluation*, *Parameter Expansion* and *Arrays* sections say evaluates its operand, whether or not (a) ran it. The existing `$((` entry stays. The verdict becomes `deny` / `uncertain` in both modes. **(c)** **The only exemptions are literal ones**, and each is backed by a shell-oracle case. An operand made only of decimal digits contains no variable, so `${x:0:7}`, `${arr[0]}` and `[[ 3 -eq 3 ]]` stay readable. The subscripts `@` and `*` (`${#arr[@]}`, `${arr[*]}`) are not arithmetic. Nothing else is exempt: `((i++))` names a variable whose value bash evaluates, so it is uncertain. **(d)** **Where it applies:** outside quotes, inside double quotes, and in an unquoted here-document body. Readiness importing policies stays inside `packages/domain`. **(g)** Backlog 509's item 4 (how often recorded runs write these forms) needs the product owner's dogfood database and is **not** a criterion. The hook lives in `apps/web/src/app/queries.ts` with the other read hooks, because `endpoint-callers.test.ts` reads it there. **(d)** **The census:** `apps/server/src/routes/client-census.test.ts` names `GET /api/org/models` in both directions, and its unauthenticated probe asserts 401. `endpoint-callers.test.ts` holds the hook to its caller. technical/08 lists the route and the retry DTO's new field. **(e)** Backlog 498's item 6, the typo's run-time outcome on a real CLI, needs a model credential. That reaches the git boundary and the block list's git entries in the image as shipped. Quote removal is already read (`g\it`, `'git'`, `"g"it` and `g""it` answer `git_boundary`), and a globbed **argument** is already refused (`git push origin ma?n`, `{main,}` and `m{a,}in` answer `git_boundary`). **519:** `read -r X <<< 'x'` is `uncertain`, and `git status <&3` and `cat <&3` are `unattended_auto` in every baseline, so both over-refuse under `deny` and neither is a reach. The implementer re-measures every form and records the before and after table in WP-160's shape. **(b)** **Backlog 515, by git's own rule.** git maps a long token to an option only when the token is a prefix of one of that option's spellings. So being a prefix of a hazardous spelling is a **necessary** condition for an abbreviation to reach a hazard, and it is the floor. A git word `--t` or `--t=v` (anywhere before a bare `--`, behind any wrapper, in every candidate the git boundary already reads) is floored at the hazard's kind whenever `t` is a prefix, of one character or more, of a hazardous spelling. There is **one exception**: git's own resolution of `t` against the subcommand's table answers exactly one option, and that option is not hazardous. Resolution means an exact spelling wins, and otherwise a unique prefix over every spelling (positive and `no-`) wins. An ambiguous `t` stays floored, which costs nothing because git refuses it. A subcommand with no table (an alias, `git-foo`, `mergetool`, `fetch-pack`, `submodule`, or anything unknown) is floored on the prefix alone. **The table can only ever remove a floor, and only by git's rule.** The hazardous spellings are the long names of the existing git entries of `HAZARDOUS_ARGUMENTS` (`no-verify`, `upload-pack`, `receive-pack`, `exec`, `extcmd`, `ext-diff`, `textconv`, `output`), with each entry's kind. The existing full-spelling globs and WP-160's `rebase -x` predicate stay, and the new predicate sits beside them. Short options are unchanged. **Rejected:** listing each shortest prefix by hand (WP-54's npm method, and 515's first option), because it drifts silently with git. 515's ≥ 4-character fallback, because `git push --ex=` ran. `GIT_TEST_DISALLOW_ABBREVIATED_OPTIONS` in the CLI's environment, because it is an undocumented test knob, an `env` prefix or `unset` removes it, it would move this row into `runner/options.ts` and WP-118's environment census, and the attended `allow` is the policy's verdict regardless. It is recorded in the row notes as possible defence in depth, and it is not built. **(c)** **The table is generated data.** `packages/domain/src/policies/git-long-options.generated.ts` is pure data with no I/O. It is written by `scripts/git-long-options.mjs`, which runs `git <sub> --git-completion-helper-all` inside a throwaway repository in the run image. It covers every subcommand that an allow, ask or block entry names, plus every subcommand whose table holds a hazardous spelling. It records `git --version`, and it gives `log`, `show` and `whatchanged` diff's spellings as **exact-only** entries, so `git log -p --text` resolves to `--text` as git does. The generator **refuses** an empty table for a subcommand it expects to have one, so a git that drops the undocumented helper fails loudly. A Docker check, `node scripts/git-long-options-check.mjs`, regenerates and diffs. Like `runlet-container-check`, it is not a `verify` target. The stated residual: a table older than the image's git can drop a floor only if git **removed** the option it resolved to. **(d)** **Backlog 516: the bound fails closed at each site.** Past `MAX_WRAPPER_DEPTH`, the argv peel (`argv0Candidates`, `commandPositions`) and the substitution loop (`parseCommand`'s `depth < MAX_WRAPPER_DEPTH` else-branch) add one new `UNCERTAINTY` entry, worded for the model: *"write the command with fewer wrappers, assignments or nested substitutions; the platform reads eight"*. WP-160's handed-string branch keeps `handedCommand`. The bound stays one constant, and an unbounded peel was considered and not chosen: the substitution recursion needs a bound anyway, and one rule is one set of cases. The attended verdict past the bound may stay `ask`, because that is a person's decision. **(e)** **Backlog 517: structure, never substring.** `gitBoundaryViolation`'s `composite` test (`packages/domain/src/policies/unattended-commands.ts:761-762`) is replaced. The whole line is skipped only when the splitter found more than one segment in it, and a fragment is skipped only when the splitter's own parts show that it is made of other fragments. No `includes` on text. **(f)** **Backlog 518: a command name the shell expands is uncertain.** The word in command position after the assignment and wrapper peel (never the peeled token itself, per 512) becomes `uncertain` under one new entry, *"write the command's name literally; the platform cannot read a name the shell expands"*, when it holds an unquoted `*`, `?` or `[` or a brace expansion with `,` or `..`. The same applies to a wrapper's own name (`/usr/bin/ni?e`). `[`, `[[`, `{ …; }`, an assignment and a brace word with neither `,` nor `..` stay certain, and WP-160's shell reading of a globbed shell name keeps its verdict. **(g)** **Backlog 519, optional.** The name reader skips a redirection's word, so `read -r X <<< 'x'` is certain. `<&N`, `>&N` and `<&-` are one redirection word, so `git status <&3` answers like `git status`. **(h)** Docs (rule 83). `HAZARDOUS_ARGUMENTS`' docblock states the abbreviation rule and git 2.47.3. `MAX_WRAPPER_DEPTH`'s docblock (`packages/domain/src/policies/command-policy.ts:1573-1576`) loses *"not fail-closed"*. **Order and machine.** Table order is the order of value. WP-150 comes first because it is money.
WP-151 follows it because the two share `spawn-adapter.ts` and WP-151's refused handshake ends through
WP-150's start-failure path. Then come WP-152, the only row that closes a side door on the record,
and WP-153. WP-153 is **parallel-safe** with WP-150, WP-154 and WP-155: it touches only
`packages/domain/src/policies/`. WP-154 and WP-155 are both SPA rows and are parallel-safe with
everything except each other, because both touch `apps/web/src/app/queries.ts`. *Amended 2026-10-06*: WP-154's (b′) server half (backlog 502) makes it run after WP-150, and not in parallel with WP-151 or WP-152, which touch the same stop and start-failure paths. WP-151 (optional
leg), WP-157 (optional leg) and WP-156 (its pg-boss measurement is integration-tier, no daemon) are
the only rows that may want Docker. **Carried, not scheduled**, with reasons: PROGRESS § "Architect
ruling (M9, session 12)".
*Amended 2026-10-06 (second amendment)*: WP-158 and WP-159 were added after WP-157 and run **before** the rows above them that are still open, in the order WP-158, WP-159, WP-154, WP-155, WP-156, WP-157. WP-158 closes an allow path under `auto` that runs a hidden command, and that is security. It needs WP-153 and touches only `packages/domain/src/policies/` and `packages/domain/src/readiness/recheck.ts`, so it is parallel-safe with every other row. WP-159 is the product owner's request. It is serial with WP-154, WP-155 and WP-157 and parallel-safe with WP-158 and WP-156. Neither row needs a migration, so the next free number is still **0087**. The reasons are in PROGRESS § "Architect ruling (M9, session 12)", second amendment.
*Amended 2026-10-07 (third amendment)*: WP-160 (backlog 511, 513 and 512) was added. It is security, as WP-158 was, so it runs **next after WP-159**, and the order of the rows still open is WP-159, WP-160, WP-154, WP-155, WP-156, WP-157. It touches only `packages/domain/src/policies/` and technical/05, so it is **parallel-safe with every open row, WP-159 included**, and it may start beside WP-159 if a second implementer is free. It depends only on WP-158, which is done. It needs no migration, so the next free number is still **0087**. The reasons are in PROGRESS § "Architect ruling (M9, session 12)", third amendment.
*Amended 2026-10-07 (fourth amendment)*: WP-161 (backlog 515, 516, 517 and 518, with 519 optional) was added. It is security, as WP-158 and WP-160 were, so it runs **next after WP-160**, and the order of the rows still open is WP-160 (in review), WP-161, WP-154, WP-155, WP-156, WP-157. It edits the same functions WP-160 edits (`command-policy.ts`, `unattended-commands.ts`, `here-documents.shell.test.ts`), so it is **serial after WP-160** and starts on WP-160's landed tree. It is **parallel-safe with WP-154…WP-157**: none of them touches `packages/domain/src/policies/`, and its new files (`scripts/git-long-options*.mjs`, the generated table) are its own. Its Docker check needs the run image (`platform-runtime:wp151` until `:dev` is rebuilt). It needs no migration, so the next free number is still **0087**. The reasons are in PROGRESS § "Architect ruling (M9, session 12)", fourth amendment.

## Milestone M10 — what M9's own rows left behind (architect, session 14)

**Why this milestone exists.** M9 is complete (WP-150…WP-161, the last at `d080c81f`). Its rows,
their reviews and the orchestrator's own sessions filed PROGRESS backlog **499**, **501**, **504–508**,
**514**, **524–530** and **532**, and none of them has an owner. M10 schedules them in the protocol's
order: what corrupts the repository the rows are built in, or holds money wrongly, comes first; then
security; then the record (a task state with no way out, and three readers that tell the wrong
story); then the screens; then onboarding; then the instruments. **No provider entry and no
operations entry is open that can be built without a model credential**, so neither class has a row
(backlog 505 and 506's count wait for a real CLI; see PROGRESS § "Architect ruling (M10, session 14)").
**No row needs a model credential, a GitLab or a Jira**: every criterion is met with fakes, the
Testcontainers database and, where a row names it, the Docker daemon. WP-33 stays BLOCKED and is not a
row. **No row is expected to need a migration.** A row that finds it needs one takes **0088**, the
next free number, and says why in its notes.

### M10 head — the tracker's lifecycle, the claim and the merge-request conversation (architect, session 15; the product owner's priority)

**Why these rows come first.** On 2026-10-08 the product owner marked PROGRESS backlog **535** (tracker
statuses), **536** (a claimed ticket) and **537** (the review conversation on the merge request) as
high priority, ahead of every other open entry. The same day, the product owner decided seven rulings,
recorded as **BD-031**. The architect's technical choices are **TD-029**. product/04, product/06,
product/17 and technical/02, /03, /06 and /12 carry the 2026-10-08 amendments.

**Order.** WP-170…WP-183 run **first**. WP-163…WP-169 follow them in their table order below. WP-162 is
DONE. One migration: **0088**, at WP-177.

**Fakes only.** No row calls the product owner's Jira or GitLab. Every fixture is shaped from vendor
documentation, carries a `source` block, and uses invented, neutral status names: *Backlog*, *Doing*,
*Waiting for review*, *Reviewed*, *Testing*, *Sent back*, *Finished*. No tracker's real status names
appear in product code, templates, defaults, prompts or tests (BD-031 ruling 1).

**What was measured before these rows were written** is in PROGRESS § "Architect ruling (M10 head,
session 15)". In short, the review window drops a general note with `resolvable: false`, and a ticket
comment never returns a task.

The marker regex exists twice, character for character: `PLATFORM_NOTE_MARKER` behind `isPlatformNote` (`packages/application/src/pipeline/review-threads.ts:70`, `:87-88`) and `MR_PLATFORM_MARKER` behind the domain's `isPlatformWord` (`packages/domain/src/lifecycle/human-return.ts:108`, `:111-115`). `isPlatformNote` has three callers that decide whose note it is: the comment handler (`saga.ts:1688`), the poll's person filter (`mr-poll.ts:611`) and the thread refresh (`review-threads-refresh.ts:72`). If the two copies drift, the window and the handlers disagree about whose note it is, so one note can return a task while it is left out of the count, or the other way round. The domain exports the merge-request test, and `isPlatformNote` delegates to it, or is replaced by it. No regex for the `<!-- agentic:` marker is left in `packages/application`. A unit case: the same bodies (a marked note, a marker after leading whitespace, a marker that is not at the start, an unmarked note) give the same answer through `isPlatformNote` and `isPlatformWord`. **Canary**: put a private copy of the regex back in `review-threads.ts` and narrow it, and the case fails. **(14)** **A pass needs the ticket to have left `qa`** (refiner, session 15, found at WP-174). `isPass` (`packages/domain/src/lifecycle/human-return.ts:146-155` in WP-174's uncommitted tree) passes when the current stage is `qa` and the status is neither the `qa` slot nor a return status. TD-029 decision 9 says a pass is the ticket **leaving** the `qa` status. Decision 4 lets a failed `qa` lifecycle write leave the stage running, so the ticket can still sit at its `approved` or `in_review` status when the task enters `qa`. The window's first firing there (an acknowledgement is enough to arm it) then answers `pass`, and QA is skipped with no person involved. WP-174 ruling (e)'s `HumanReturnInput` (`:80-91`) carries only the status now, so the domain cannot tell the two apart. **Decided:** `HumanReturnInput` gains `leftQa: string \| null`. It is the `to` of the newest `ticket.status.changed` (`from`/`to`, `packages/contracts/src/events.ts:212-218`) recorded for the task after its latest entry into `qa` whose `from` matches the `qa` slot (`lifecycleStatusKey`), else `null`. `isPass` also requires `leftQa !== null` and that it is not a return status. Without that event the task waits at `qa`. That is the safe direction: one person moves the ticket again. **Needs measurement:** whether the poll-only path (WP-110's `ticket.updated` re-read) ever records the previous status. If it does not, a poll-only binding can never pass `qa` by status, and the setup guide says so. A case: the task enters `qa` with the ticket at the `approved` status (the `qa` write refused), and an acknowledgement fires the window. The answer is `none`, not `pass`. Then a `ticket.status.changed` from the `qa` slot to `Done` answers `pass`. **Canary**: drop the `leftQa` clause, and the first half fails. **(15)** **Whose ticket comment: markers at the start only** (refiner, session 15, found at WP-174). `isPlatformWord` (`human-return.ts:111-115`) treats a ticket comment as the platform's when `PLATFORM_COMMENT_MARKERS` (`packages/domain/src/ask/ask.ts:141-146`) appear **anywhere** (`includes`), while TD-029 decision 6 says the body **opens with** one. The ask mirror puts its marker mid-line: `**Asked and answered** — ${askCommentMarker(ask.id)}` (`packages/application/src/ask/mirror.ts:69`). Cost of leaving it: a person who quotes a platform comment while asking for a change is silenced. That is the failure direction decision 8 rejects. **Decided:** markers at the start only, as decision 6 says. The test matches the merge-request side (optional leading whitespace, then the marker). `renderAskComment` moves its marker to the first line (the visible heading follows it). The ask classifier's own `includes` (`ask.ts:203`) is **coupled**: it uses the same list, and `ask.test.ts:95` pins a mid-body marker. This row narrows it the same way, or states in TD-029 decision 6 why the classifier keeps `includes` (there a false *platform* reading costs one unanswered `@agentic ask`, not a lost return). Mirror comments posted before this row keep the old shape, and they are older than any horizon the window reads in practice. Say so in the docblock. A case: a person's comment that quotes a whole mirror comment, followed by *please rename X*, returns the task at `qa` and at `ready_for_merge`. A comment that opens with the marker does not. **Canary**: restore `includes`, and the quoting case fails. Criterion (13)'s one-copy rule applies to this test too. **(16)** **The dependency gate's post-review set includes `qa`** (refiner, session 15). Ruling (a)'s list of sites leaves out `packages/application/src/pipeline/dependency-gate.ts`. `PAST_REVIEW_STAGES` (`:736-741`: `ready_for_merge`, `merged_gate`, retro and librarian) decides Q91's ending: a dependency question on a task that has passed review is refused, and the record is kept (`:901-904`, the log lines at `:935` and `:1123`). It is read only when the task is **not** `active` (`:920-922`). The state machine has no `qa` state (`packages/domain/src/aggregates/task-state-machine.ts:80-89`), so a task waiting at `qa` is presumably `active`. **Confirm this when building.** If it is, the gate's `ask` ending calls `askNow` and puts a blocking question to a task waiting on a human tester. That breaks Q91. Widening means three things. **(i)** `qa` joins `PAST_REVIEW_STAGES`, so a task paused at `qa` is refused rather than deferred. **(ii)** The `active` branch also refuses when `current_stage` is `qa`. **(iii)** The deferred-resume path (`:1046`) drops a deferred `ask` whose resume lands at `qa`. The `block` ending is unchanged: `returnNow` (`:850-884`) returns from whatever stage it reads, which already includes `ready_for_merge`. The docblocks at `:726-734`, `:901-904` and `:1046` name `qa`. A case: the gate's `ask` ending fires for a task at `qa`, and no `questions` row is written while the panel's record says *not asked*. **Canary**: remove `qa` from the set and the guard, and a question is inserted. **Parallel-safety:** serial after WP-177 and before WP-179. |

| WP-162 | **A test never writes into the repository it runs in, and the harness's own files do not turn `verify` red** (backlog **499**, **532**). **Migration: no.** **Rulings:** **(a)** **The cause is measured, not assumed** (this pass, git 2.54.0, plain git hooks, a throwaway repository with a local bare remote, never this one). A linked worktree's `pre-push` hook runs with `GIT_DIR=<common>/.git/worktrees/<name>` in its environment, and the main checkout's hook has no `GIT_DIR`. A hook that runs `git init .` in a fresh scratch directory and commits and tags there **set `core.bare=true` in the shared configuration, created the tag in the shared refs and moved the worktree's `HEAD` onto the fixture commit**, while the scratch directory received only the file. So the fixtures inherited `GIT_DIR` and acted on the repository the worktree shares. Lefthook was not measured. **(b)** **One helper** builds every git child's environment for a scratch repository, in a module both the `.ts` tests and the `.mjs` scripts they spawn can import (`scripts/git-scratch-env.mjs`). It drops **every** inherited name with the `GIT_` prefix, by prefix and not by a list. It then adds back the fixture's own values: `GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL` and `GIT_CONFIG_SYSTEM` set to `/dev/null`, the author and committer, `core.hooksPath=/dev/null` through `GIT_CONFIG_COUNT`, and `GIT_CEILING_DIRECTORIES` set to the scratch root's parent, so discovery cannot climb out of a directory that is not yet a repository. After `git init` the helper asserts that `git rev-parse --absolute-git-dir` is inside the scratch root, and throws before the first write otherwise. Every site backlog 499 lists uses it, including the scripts the tests spawn (`check-*.mjs`, `census-files.mjs`, `version.mjs`, `changelog.mjs`) and `packages/infrastructure/src/workspace/tracked.test.ts`'s shell script. **The production scripts are not changed**: in a hook they are meant to act on the repository git names. **(c)** **A census** refuses a child-process call that runs `git` (a literal command name, or one of the scripts above) in any test file of the unit, process, integration or e2e tiers whose options do not take the helper's environment. It reads files through `scripts/census-files.mjs`, tracked and untracked. Its docblock lists what it cannot see: a `git` inside a shell string, and a command name held in a variable. **(d)** **The reproduction runs in CI.** A process-tier suite (added to `PROCESS_SUITES`, which `scripts/verify.test.ts` holds) creates a throwaway repository, points `GIT_DIR`, `GIT_INDEX_FILE` and `GIT_WORK_TREE` at it in a child's environment, and runs the git-fixture suites in that child with coverage off (two vitest runs in one checkout collide on the coverage directory). It asserts that the throwaway's `config`, `HEAD`, loose and packed refs and `index` are byte-identical afterwards. **(e)** Standing rule 95 is retired in the same change, by the orchestrator, and technical/14's worktree parallelism applies again. **(f)** **Backlog 532**: a set of **root-anchored** local-tool paths beside `OS_ARTEFACT_NAMES` (`scripts/os-artefacts.mjs:27`): `.claude/scheduled_tasks.lock`, `.claude/scheduled_tasks.json`, `.claude/settings.local.json`, and the directories `.claude/routines/.state/`, `.claude/checkpoints/` and `.claude/mailbox/`. `scripts/check-ignored.mjs` consults it only for **untracked** walk results (the branch at `:181`), so a tracked path is checked whatever its name and a `scheduled_tasks.lock` anywhere else still fails. Its docblock says the list follows the harness, and that a new harness file turns local `verify` red until it is added, which is the loud direction. The source filter (report only a committed `.gitignore`'s matches) is rejected: it silences every private exclude, which is the class the guard exists for. | — | **(1)** The implementer repeats (a) under **lefthook** in a throwaway clone with a linked worktree, records each `GIT_*` name and value in the row notes, and runs `scripts/census-files.test.ts` from that hook on today's tree and on the fixed tree, recording what changed in the throwaway each time. Never in this repository. **(2)** The helper has unit cases: a parent environment carrying `GIT_DIR`, `GIT_INDEX_FILE`, `GIT_WORK_TREE`, `GIT_COMMON_DIR` and an invented `GIT_FUTURE_NAME` yields none of them, and the `rev-parse` guard throws for a root whose git directory is elsewhere. **Canary**: replace the prefix rule with a list of the four known names, and the invented-name case fails. **(3)** The census fails on a planted unscrubbed `git` spawn in a scratch test file, tracked and untracked. **Canary**: revert one migrated site, and the census names it. **(4)** The (d) suite fails on today's tree (the canary is that run, recorded in the notes) and passes on the fixed tree. **(5)** `check-ignored` unit cases: the lock and `settings.local.json` are exempt; `apps/server/src/scheduled_tasks.lock` is not; a tracked `.claude/agents/*.md` matched by a planted pattern still fails. **Canary**: empty the set, and the lock is reported again. **(6)** `pnpm run -s verify` reads `PASS` on this machine **with** the harness lock present, recorded with the lock's existence shown. **(7)** CLAUDE.md's commands paragraph and the session-12 workaround sentence in the ledger are amended as history (rule 83) |
| WP-163 | **A stop that lands while the spawn marker commits holds no money, and a stop the platform made logs no fault** (backlog **501**, **506**). **Migration: no.** **Rulings:** **(a)** **The adapter tells the record what it knows.** `sendSpawn` (`packages/infrastructure/src/runlet/spawn-adapter.ts:384-403`) awaits the gate at `:388` and returns at `:398-400`, before `spawnSent = true`, when the shim exited, the signal aborted, the process was killed or the connection is gone. On that return, and only after a gate that passed, it calls a second start hook, `cliSpawnWithheld`, exactly once. It never calls it after a frame was sent. **(b)** `CliSpawnRecord` (`packages/application/src/pipeline/cli-spawn.ts:55-106`) gains the withheld fact, and **one predicate replaces `requested()` in its public interface**: *may have reached the CLI*, which is `written && !withheld`. Every executor site that reads `requested()` today reads the predicate (`packages/application/src/pipeline/stage-executor.ts:1299`, `:1375`, `:1382`; `packages/application/src/ask/executor.ts:960`, `:985`, `:1020`), and because the old name is gone, the compiler is the census. **(c)** The ending is the unspawned one. A stop goes through `unspawnedStop` with a new step word, `spawn_withheld`, in `UnspawnedStopStep` (`cli-spawn.ts:144`) and platform text naming it. Any other ending is a start failure with `ended_before_cli_spawn`. The cost is `MEASURED_ZERO_COST` (`cli-spawn.ts:52`), written to `usd_reported`, so `unmeasuredEndedRunSql` (`packages/infrastructure/src/cost/pending-run-spend.ts:72-75`) does not hold the run. **The marker is not cleared.** `runs.cli_spawn_requested_at` stays the true record that the platform asked, and `exit_detail` says that the frame was withheld. **Order and machine.** Table order is the order of value. **WP-162 comes first.** It is the only
major entry open, and until it lands standing rule 95 forbids a tier or a push from a linked worktree,
so no two rows can run in parallel. **WP-163** is money. It is parallel-safe with WP-162, which
touches only `scripts/` and test files, so the two may start together on the main checkout if two
implementers are free, with their `verify` runs sequenced, because two unit tiers in one checkout collide on vitest's coverage directory. **WP-164** is security and touches
only `packages/domain/src/policies/` and technical/05. It is parallel-safe with every row. **WP-165**
and **WP-167** both touch `commands.ts` and `pipeline-queries.ts`, so they are serial. **WP-167** and
**WP-168** both touch `packages/contracts/src/api.ts` and `apps/web/src/app/queries.ts`, so they are
serial. **WP-166** is parallel-safe with every row. **WP-169** runs after WP-162. Only WP-169's (a) and
(c) want the Docker daemon. No row needs a migration. **Carried, not scheduled**, with reasons: PROGRESS
§ "Architect ruling (M10, session 14)".

## Suggested agent team for the orchestrator
- **Architect (this role, read-only)** for questions about decisions; **Backend developer** (domain/application/infrastructure), **Integrations developer** (providers + contract tests), **Runner/security developer** (SDK runner, launcher, isolation), **Frontend developer** (SPA), **Prompt engineer** (roles, evals), **Reviewer** (fresh context, security checklist), **Docs writer**. Sequence M1 WPs by dependencies; WP-13 early because the whole runner path depends on it.

## Definition of done (per WP)
Tests per technical/10 green in CI; coverage thresholds met; contract/golden fixtures added; docs updated; no new `[unverified]` claims in docs without a TODO entry; reviewer approval; conventional commits with DCO. A row that changes a screen under `apps/web/src/features/` sweeps `docs/user-guide.md` for the sentences it falsified (rule 83; added by the M7 pass, backlog 400).
